# mdmate

## Problem

Good inline Markdown editing is tied to heavy note-taking apps or locked inside other editors. Fast editors like SublimeText show raw Markdown, and viewers can't edit. There is no small desktop app that opens a Markdown file instantly, renders it where you type, and keeps the file on disk exactly as you wrote it.

## Vision

You double-click a README and it is on screen before you have finished looking at it, rendered and ready to type in. The syntax appears only where your caret is. Tabs, a folder sidebar and free splits feel like SublimeText, and a 1 MB file feels the same as a short note. Rename a file and every link to it follows. Close the app mid-sentence, open it tomorrow, and your unsaved words are still there, and git shows only the lines you meant to change.

## Out of Scope

- **Math and Mermaid.** Later, as separately loaded chunks.
- **Wiki links.** Standard Markdown links only.
- **Multi-cursor.** Ctrl+click is reserved for links.
- **A reading mode.** Live preview and raw source cover it.
- **Spellcheck.** Left out of v1.
- **Table cell editing.** Tables are read-only until the caret reveals their source.
- **Multi-window.** One instance, one window.
- **Auto-update, accounts, telemetry.** None in v1.
- **A visual theme editor.** Themes are files.
- **Export to HTML or PDF, plugins, sync, AI features.** Different products.
- **macOS and Linux installers in v1.** The code stays portable; only Windows ships.

## Principles

- The file on disk belongs to the writer. The app displays Markdown and never rewrites it.
- Speed is a feature with numbers, measured on real files.
- A document someone else wrote is untrusted input.
- Vue owns the chrome and CodeMirror owns the text.
- Tools enforce what they can; prose holds what they can't.

## Constraints

- Tauri 2, Rust, Vue 3, TypeScript, Tailwind 4, shadcn-vue on Reka UI, Vite, pnpm. `derived_from: principal_stated_goal compound`
- CodeMirror 6 written from scratch. The Markdown text is the document; no parse-and-serialize model.
- All colors, fonts and spacing come from design tokens.
- All OS-specific code lives behind `src-tauri/src/platform/`.
- All file access goes through our own Rust commands.
- Windows 11 ships first, on WebView2.
- **Speed gate.** Four speed claims are gates: ISC-4 (startup 1500 ms), ISC-5 (open 1 MB, 100 ms), ISC-6 (tab switch, 50 ms) and ISC-7 (typing p95 16 ms on the 1 MB fixture). When a gate is measured and missed in the release build, the work stops at that task. Profile once to find the cost, then write the numbers and the cause in the phase's `decisions.md` and hand them to the owner, who decides between a fix, a lower feature scope (such as a lower plain-source cutoff) or a new target. A gate is never loosened, skipped or re-measured under easier conditions to make it pass. Only the owner closes a missed gate, with the decision recorded in Decisions.

## Goal

"Create a markdown editor that follows the inline editing philosophy from Obsidian and the simplicity of SublimeText". mdmate is a desktop app where Markdown renders inline and reveals its syntax at the caret, with tabs, a sidebar and free splits in the SublimeText style. It meets its measured speed targets, keeps files byte-exact, and is built so macOS and Linux need only new platform implementations.

## Not yet specified


## Test Strategy

Commands: `pnpm test:rust <filter>` runs Cargo tests, `pnpm test:web <file>` runs Vitest, `pnpm perf <scenario>` runs the release-build harness, and `node scripts/check-arch.mjs <rule>` runs structural checks. Web UI rows use Interceptor against `pnpm dev`; real-app rows use `pnpm tauri dev`.

## Features

### F0 · Cross-cutting
Why: these promises hold everywhere, and any single feature could quietly break them.

- [x] ISC-1: Anti: saving an unedited file never changes a byte.
- [x] ISC-2: Anti: an edit never changes bytes outside its range.
- [x] ISC-3: Mixed line-ending files keep each untouched line's ending after an edit.
- [x] ISC-4: The release app is usable within 1500 ms of launch.
- [x] ISC-5: A 1 MB Markdown file opens within 100 ms.
- [x] ISC-6: Switching tabs completes within 50 ms.
- [x] ISC-7: Typing stays under 16 ms per key on the 1 MB fixture.
- [x] ISC-8: Files above 3 MB open as plain source with a notice.
- [x] ISC-9: `cfg(windows)` and `target_os` appear only inside `platform/`.
- [x] ISC-10: `cargo check` passes on macOS in CI.
- [x] ISC-11: `cargo check` passes on Linux in CI.
- [x] ISC-12: `@tauri-apps` imports appear only inside `src/platform/`.
- [x] ISC-13: `src/editor/` imports nothing from Vue.
- [x] ISC-14: No hard-coded colors exist outside token files.
- [ ] ISC-15: Anti: `innerHTML` and `v-html` never receive document content.
- [x] ISC-16: The opener refuses every scheme except `http`, `https` and `mailto`.
- [x] ISC-17: Anti: UNC and `file://host/` targets never reach the filesystem.
- [x] ISC-18: Default-app opens ask first unless the file is Markdown or an image.
- [ ] ISC-19: Anti: the hostile fixture causes no code run, launch or SMB attempt.
- [ ] ISC-20: Anti: the app makes no network request except document images.
- [x] ISC-21: Anti: `tauri-plugin-fs` is absent from the dependencies.
- [x] ISC-22: `pnpm check` exits 0.
- [x] ISC-23: `pnpm check:rust` exits 0.
- [x] ISC-24: Anti: no lint suppression lacks a reason comment.
- [ ] ISC-173: Anti: an opened workspace can never supply a theme file.

### F1 · Sublime workbench
Why: a dense keyboard-driven layout you can rearrange freely is what separates this from a note app.

- [x] ISC-25: Antecedent: first launch shows titlebar, sidebar, tabs and status bar together.
- [x] ISC-26: Titlebar controls minimize, maximize and close the window.
- [x] ISC-27: Anti: launch never shows a white frame.
- [x] ISC-28: Split right creates side-by-side panes.
- [x] ISC-29: Split down creates stacked panes.
- [x] ISC-30: Splits nest three levels in mixed directions.
- [x] ISC-31: Closing a pane's last tab removes that pane.
- [x] ISC-32: Dragging a tab moves it to another pane.
- [x] ISC-33: Dragging a tab to a pane edge creates a split.
- [x] ISC-34: Dragging a split handle resizes the adjacent panes.
- [x] ISC-35: A keyboard shortcut toggles the sidebar.
- [x] ISC-171: A keyboard shortcut splits the active pane right.
- [x] ISC-172: A keyboard shortcut splits the active pane down.
- [x] ISC-176: Dragging a tab within a strip reorders it.

### F2 · Reliable documents
Why: nobody trusts an editor twice after it loses their words, so no action here may lose work.

- [x] ISC-36: Opening a file loads its content into a tab.
- [x] ISC-37: Ctrl+S writes edits to disk.
- [x] ISC-38: Tabs with unsaved edits show a dirty marker.
- [x] ISC-39: Files in unsupported encodings open read-only with a notice.
- [x] ISC-40: Anti: a failed save never damages the original file.
- [x] ISC-41: An edit in one pane appears in another pane showing that file.
- [x] ISC-42: Undo from either pane reverts the last edit.
- [x] ISC-43: Closing a pane keeps the document's undo history.
- [x] ISC-44: A disk change reloads a clean tab.
- [x] ISC-45: A disk change on an edited tab raises a conflict choice.
- [x] ISC-46: Anti: mdmate's own save triggers no reload or conflict.
- [x] ISC-47: A force-kill loses nothing up to the last journal write.
- [x] ISC-48: A restart restores tabs and split layout.
- [x] ISC-49: Anti: closing the app with unsaved edits shows no prompt.
- [x] ISC-174: Opening an already open file focuses its tab, and a file is not opened twice within one pane.

### F3 · Inline live preview
Why: this is the reason mdmate exists; you read the rendered page and edit it in place, with syntax only where you are working.

- [x] ISC-50: Headings render without `#` markers away from the caret.
- [x] ISC-51: [container] Emphasis renders with hidden markers.
  - [x] ISC-51.1: Bold renders with hidden markers.
  - [x] ISC-51.2: Italic renders with hidden markers.
  - [x] ISC-51.3: Strikethrough renders with hidden markers.
- [x] ISC-52: Inline code renders with hidden backticks.
- [x] ISC-53: Links render as text with the target hidden.
- [x] ISC-54: List markers render styled.
- [x] ISC-55: Blockquotes render styled.
- [x] ISC-56: Horizontal rules render as lines.
- [x] ISC-57: A caret inside an inline element reveals only that element's syntax.
- [x] ISC-58: Syntax hides again within 16 ms of the caret leaving.
- [x] ISC-59: Anti: the caret never sticks inside hidden syntax.
- [x] ISC-60: Anti: vertical arrow keys never skip a line.
- [x] ISC-61: A per-pane toggle shows raw source.
- [x] ISC-62: Copying a selection copies raw Markdown.
- [x] ISC-63: Decoration time stays flat between 1 MB and 10 MB files.

### F4 · Block preview
Why: documents mix prose with tables, images and code, and a page full of raw syntax blocks breaks the illusion.

- [x] ISC-64: GFM tables render read-only.
- [x] ISC-65: A caret entering a table reveals its raw source.
- [x] ISC-66: Local images render inline from relative paths.
- [x] ISC-67: Remote `https` images render inline.
- [x] ISC-68: YAML frontmatter collapses to a pill.
- [x] ISC-69: Clicking a checkbox changes exactly one byte.
- [x] ISC-70: Fenced code highlights using token colors.
- [x] ISC-71: The entry bundle holds no language parser beyond the ones Markdown itself imports (HTML, CSS and JavaScript); every other language loads when a fence names it.
- [x] ISC-72: Anti: late-rendering widgets never shift the scroll position.
- [x] ISC-180: Anti: an image address other than a relative path (inside the document folder, or inside its workspace folder, ISC-232) or `https` never loads.
- [x] ISC-181: Opening a document lets the page load images from its folder, and from no other folder; a workspace folder adds its own folder (and below) when it is opened, and nothing else does.
- [x] ISC-182: A broken or still-loading image holds a fixed-size placeholder.
- [x] ISC-183: Anti: no selection head ever sits strictly inside a block replacement.
- [x] ISC-184: Anti: a read-only document ignores a checkbox click.
- [x] ISC-185: A fenced code block is drawn as a bordered, rounded container in the light and the dark theme.
- [x] ISC-186: With the caret outside a fenced code block, its fences are hidden and its language shows as a label at the top right.
- [x] ISC-187: With the caret inside a fenced code block, edges included, its fences show and the label is gone.
- [x] ISC-188: Anti: a fenced code block never changes height when the caret enters or leaves it.
- [x] ISC-189: The container is one module that a block of another kind (Mermaid, later) can use.
- [x] ISC-73: Anti: `katex` and `mermaid` are absent from v1 dependencies.

### F5 · Link navigation
Why: documentation is a web of files, so following a link has to cost one gesture and be undoable.

- [x] ISC-74: Ctrl+click on a relative `.md` link opens it in a new tab.
- [x] ISC-75: Ctrl+click on a `#heading` link jumps to that heading.
- [x] ISC-76: Ctrl+click on `file.md#heading` opens the file at that heading.
- [x] ISC-77: Ctrl+click on a web link opens the default browser.
- [x] ISC-78: A confirmed link to another file opens its default app.
- [x] ISC-79: Links with missing targets show a broken style.
- [x] ISC-80: Alt+Left returns to the previous location.
- [x] ISC-81: Alt+Right moves forward again.
- [x] ISC-82: Anti: a plain click on a link never navigates.

### F6 · Quick editing
Why: formatting should stay in your fingers, so writing never stops for syntax.

- [x] ISC-83: The bold shortcut toggles bold.
- [x] ISC-84: The italic shortcut toggles italic.
- [x] ISC-85: The link shortcut inserts a link.
- [x] ISC-86: The inline code shortcut toggles inline code.
- [x] ISC-87: The strikethrough shortcut toggles strikethrough.
- [x] ISC-88: A shortcut raises the heading level.
- [x] ISC-89: A shortcut lowers the heading level.
- [x] ISC-90: Enter continues a list.
- [x] ISC-91: Enter continues a checkbox list with an unchecked box.
- [x] ISC-92: Enter on an empty item ends the list.
- [x] ISC-93: Tab indents a list item.
- [x] ISC-94: Shift+Tab outdents a list item.
- [x] ISC-95: Pasting a URL over selected text creates a link.
- [x] ISC-96: Anti: the editor never holds more than one selection range.
- [x] ISC-177: Dragging selected text to another position in the editor moves it.
- [x] ISC-178: A text drag undoes in one step.
- [x] ISC-179: Text drag works in the real app while the window accepts native file drops.
- [x] ISC-190: Anti: a paste never changes a read-only document.
- [x] ISC-191: A drawn block is never a drop target.

### F7 · Workspace
Why: Markdown lives among other files, so the sidebar must manage them and renames must never strand a link.

- [x] ISC-97: Multiple root folders show in the tree.
- [x] ISC-98: A loose file opens without any root folder.
- [x] ISC-99: A 10,000-entry folder renders under 200 rows.
- [x] ISC-100: The tree creates a new file.
- [x] ISC-101: The tree renames a file.
- [x] ISC-102: Dragging in the tree moves a file.
- [x] ISC-103: Deleting moves a file to the Recycle Bin, once the owner has confirmed it (ISC-227).
- [x] ISC-104: Reveal in Explorer selects the file.
- [x] ISC-105: Non-Markdown files show dimmed.
- [x] ISC-106: Other text files open as plain source.
- [x] ISC-107: Images open in an image tab.
- [x] ISC-108: The image tab fits a large image to the pane.
- [x] ISC-109: The image tab zooms.
- [x] ISC-110: The image tab pans.
- [x] ISC-111: The image tab shows dimensions and file size.
- [x] ISC-112: Anti: scripts in an SVG never run.
- [x] ISC-113: After a rename, a rescan finds zero links to the old path in the workspace.
- [x] ISC-114: Moving a file updates the relative links inside it.
- [x] ISC-115: Renaming a folder updates links to files within it.
- [x] ISC-116: Link rewrite covers reference definitions.
- [x] ISC-117: Link rewrite covers `<img src>` tags.
- [x] ISC-118: Link rewrite covers percent-encoded targets.
- [x] ISC-119: Link rewrite covers angle-bracket targets.
- [x] ISC-120: Link rewrite preserves `#anchor` suffixes.
- [x] ISC-121: Link rewrite covers folders excluded from the tree. (Since Phase 7.1 it does not enter the built-in excluded folders of ISC-282, which hold build and dependency output; ISC-294 says so for every scan. A folder the tree hides for any other reason is still covered.)
- [x] ISC-122: Link rewrite writes closed files directly.
- [x] ISC-123: Link rewrite gives dirty tabs undoable, unsaved edits.
- [x] ISC-124: Link rewrite covers other text files. (Since 2026-11-11 it covers only Markdown files, by ISC-253.)
- [x] ISC-175: Link rewrite reloads clean open tabs.
- [x] ISC-125: Anti: link rewrite never changes bytes outside link targets.
- [x] ISC-126: A rename shows the number of links and files first. (Since P7.1-T5 the question comes before any file is read, so it has no counts; the counts come after, as a line saying what was updated: ISC-316 and ISC-318.)
- [x] ISC-127: `links.confirmRewrite: false` skips the prompt. (Since P7.1-T5 the setting is `links.onRename`: `update` is the old `false`, `ask` the old `true`, and `never` looks for no links at all; ISC-315 and ISC-316.)
- [x] ISC-128: Files that fail to rewrite are listed.
- [x] ISC-129: Anti: a rewrite scan never pushes typing past 16 ms.
- [x] ISC-130: Ctrl+P finds a file by partial name.
- [x] ISC-131: Ctrl+Shift+P runs a command by name.
- [x] ISC-132: A file created outside mdmate appears in the tree.

### F8 · Search and outline
Why: long documents and folders are only usable when you can jump straight to the right place.

- [x] ISC-133: Ctrl+F finds text in the file.
- [x] ISC-134: Ctrl+H replaces text in the file.
- [x] ISC-135: Find supports regular expressions.
- [x] ISC-136: Find supports case-sensitive matching.
- [x] ISC-137: Ctrl+Shift+F searches every root folder.
- [x] ISC-138: Anti: a folder search never pushes typing past 16 ms.
- [x] ISC-139: Clicking a search result opens the file at the match.
- [x] ISC-140: Starting a new search stops the previous one.
- [x] ISC-141: The outline panel lists the file's headings.
- [x] ISC-142: Clicking an outline heading jumps there.
- [x] ISC-143: The outline reaches the last heading of the 10 MB fixture.
- [x] ISC-144: The outline reflects unsaved heading edits.

### F9 · Configuration and themes
Why: the look must be yours to change without rebuilding, which is why everything runs on tokens.

- [ ] ISC-145: Dark and light built-in themes switch live.
- [ ] ISC-146: Anti: built-in themes copy no SublimeText assets.
- [ ] ISC-147: Saving `settings.json` applies changes without a restart.
- [ ] ISC-148: Invalid settings keep the last good values with a notice.
- [ ] ISC-149: Saving `keybindings.json` changes a shortcut.
- [ ] ISC-150: A user theme file overrides a token.
- [ ] ISC-151: Saving a theme file updates the app live.
- [ ] ISC-152: The portable marker keeps data beside the exe.
- [ ] ISC-153: Common toggles run from the command palette.

### F10 · Image authoring
Why: screenshots are the most common thing dropped into documentation, and adding one should take a single paste.

- [ ] ISC-154: A pasted image saves to an images folder next to the document (`assets`, created on the first paste), as `image-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.png`.
- [ ] ISC-155: A pasted image inserts a relative link.
- [ ] ISC-156: A dropped image inserts a link at the drop point.
- [ ] ISC-157: A drag handle on a selected, loaded image (block or inline, local or `https`) resizes it: width only, aspect ratio kept, from 24 px up to the editor content width.
- [ ] ISC-158: A resized Markdown image `![alt](src)` is written as `<img src alt width>`; a resized `<img>` has its `width` set.
- [ ] ISC-159: Anti: a resize changes bytes only inside that image's source (and, for an `<img>` with a `height`, only that attribute besides `width`).
- [ ] ISC-160: Unresized images stay standard `![]()` syntax.
- [ ] ISC-322: A click on an image selects it (outline and resize handle) and its source stays hidden; Escape or a click elsewhere deselects, and Enter or an arrow key into it shows its source.
- [ ] ISC-323: An `<img>` tag with `src`, `alt`, `title`, `width` and `height` draws as an image through the same address allowlist as `![]()` (ISC-180); a tag with any other attribute shows its allowed ones only, and other HTML stays source.
- [ ] ISC-324: Anti: an `<img>` with a refused address, a `style`, or an `on*` attribute loads nothing from that address and runs no script.
- [ ] ISC-325: Delete or Backspace on a selected image removes its whole source, as one undo step.
- [ ] ISC-326: Resizing an `<img>` that has a `height` removes that `height` and changes nothing else in the tag.
- [ ] ISC-327: A paste or a drop-copy into an untitled document saves the image in that document's own draft folder (`assets/image-….png` under it) and inserts the normal relative link, which draws the image; until P9-T5 builds this, such a paste is refused with a notice and writes nothing.
- [ ] ISC-328: An image dropped on document text links in place when the file is inside the document's workspace folder, and is copied into the images folder and linked there when it is outside.
- [ ] ISC-329: Anti: a pasted or copied image never overwrites an existing file; a taken name gets `-2`, `-3`.
- [ ] ISC-330: `settings.json` holds `images.pasteFolder` (default `assets`) and a saved change applies to the next paste with no restart.
- [ ] ISC-331: The first Save As of an untitled document copies its draft images into the images folder beside the saved file and then removes the draft folder; the links already resolve there, and a name that is taken there gets `-2`, `-3` with its link updated.
- [ ] ISC-332: Closing an untitled tab removes its draft folder once the close is final (after "Don't Save", or at once for a tab with no changes), by every route that closes a tab; quitting the app keeps it, since the session restores the tab. Anti: a draft folder an open or restorable tab holds is never removed, and a folder whose tab is gone (a failed removal, a crash) is removed at the next start-up.
- [ ] ISC-333: Anti: the asset protocol is granted a draft folder only for the untitled tab that owns it, and an image link in an untitled document that leaves its draft folder never loads.

### F11 · Release
Why: it only becomes your everyday Markdown app once it opens from a double-click and strangers can install it.

- [ ] ISC-161: A second launch with a file opens a tab in the first instance.
- [ ] ISC-162: A relative path resolves against the second launch's working directory.
- [ ] ISC-163: `mdmate file.md` opens the file on a cold launch.
- [ ] ISC-164: The installer's opt-in sets the `.md` association.
- [ ] ISC-165: Anti: the installer changes no association without the opt-in.
- [ ] ISC-166: `pnpm tauri build` produces an NSIS installer.
- [ ] ISC-167: The portable zip runs from any folder.
- [ ] ISC-168: A version tag publishes a GitHub Release with both artifacts.
- [x] ISC-169: The repo carries an MIT `LICENSE`.
- [ ] ISC-170: The README lets a stranger install and use mdmate.

### F12 · File lifecycle
Why: an everyday editor starts, keeps and manages files without sending you to Explorer, and files arrive by drag as often as by dialog.

- [x] ISC-192: Open Folder adds the chosen folder as a workspace root.
- [x] ISC-193: Removing a root takes it out of the tree and revokes its access.
- [x] ISC-194: Workspace roots are restored on restart.
- [x] ISC-195: A folder dropped from Explorer outside the tree becomes a workspace root.
- [x] ISC-196: The tree creates a new folder.
- [x] ISC-197: Files dropped from Explorer on a tree folder are copied into it.
- [x] ISC-198: A folder dropped from Explorer on a tree folder is copied whole.
- [x] ISC-199: A name clash on a copy offers Replace, Keep both and Skip.
- [x] ISC-200: Markdown and text files dropped from Explorer open in tabs in the pane they land on.
- [x] ISC-201: An image dropped from Explorer outside a document's text opens in an image tab.
- [x] ISC-202: Anti: a path that no native drop delivered is never opened or copied.
- [x] ISC-203: Ctrl+N opens an untitled tab.
- [x] ISC-204: The first save of an untitled tab writes UTF-8 with LF to the path chosen in the save dialog.
- [x] ISC-205: The save dialog suggests a file name from the first heading.
- [x] ISC-206: Unsaved untitled tabs survive a restart.
- [x] ISC-207: Save As writes to the chosen path and the tab follows the new file.
- [x] ISC-208: Anti: Save As never changes the original file.
- [x] ISC-209: Save As on a read-only document writes UTF-8 and the tab becomes editable.
- [x] ISC-210: Revert File discards unsaved edits and reloads the file after a confirm.
- [x] ISC-211: Close All closes every tab, asking once for each dirty document (of its own pane only, see ISC-307).
- [x] ISC-212: Open Recent lists the last 10 files and 5 folders across restarts.
- [x] ISC-213: Close Others closes every other tab in the pane.
- [x] ISC-214: Copy Path copies the file's absolute path.
- [x] ISC-215: Copy Relative Path copies the path from its root, or the absolute path outside every root.
- [x] ISC-216: Reveal in Sidebar selects the file in the tree.
- [x] ISC-217: F2 renames the active file through the link-rewrite flow.
- [x] ISC-218: Find File References lists every file that links to the active file.
- [x] ISC-219: Anti: renaming or moving a file, or a folder holding open files, in the tree never leaves an open document on the old path; its tab, its saves and its disk watch follow the file.
- [x] ISC-220: Rename in the tab menu is off for a file outside every open folder, and a hover shows a themed shadcn tooltip (never a native title) that says why.
- [x] ISC-221: Shift+F12 runs Find File References for the active file.
- [x] ISC-222: Anti: no tooltip in the app is a native `title` attribute; every tooltip is the themed shadcn Tooltip.
- [x] ISC-223: Anti: a right click on a tab never fails to open the tab menu, with or without a tooltip showing on that tab.
- [x] ISC-224: A tab shows its tooltip only when its name is cut off, and a tab, a tree file and a tree folder wait 1 s before showing one; every other hint keeps 300 ms.
- [x] ISC-225: Anti: the tooltip arrow never draws over the tooltip box.
- [x] ISC-226: Anti: no tooltip drawn inside the editor is a native `title` attribute; a broken link, the frontmatter pill and an image that did not load show a themed tooltip that follows dark and light.
- [x] ISC-227: Deleting a file or a folder from the tree, by the menu or the Delete key, asks first in a themed shadcn dialog, and nothing is deleted until the owner confirms.
- [x] ISC-228: Confirming a delete closes every tab of the deleted file, or under the deleted folder, even one with unsaved edits; the dialog says how many tabs close and whether any is unsaved.
- [x] ISC-229: The tree menu has Copy Path and Copy Relative Path for a file, a folder and (path only) a workspace folder, and Find File References for a file.
- [x] ISC-230: The reveal item reads Open Containing Folder for a file (everywhere, tab menu included) and Open Folder for a folder, and Open Folder opens that folder itself.
- [x] ISC-231: The sidebar is resized by dragging its right edge between 12rem and half the window; hiding and showing it, and a restart, keep the width; a double click resets it.
- [x] ISC-232: An image whose relative path climbs out of the document folder but stays inside the workspace folder loads; a path out of the workspace folder (or out of the folder of a document in no workspace folder) never loads.
- [x] ISC-233: An image tab keeps its zoom and pan while the owner works in other tabs, and a tab never zoomed still fits.
- [x] ISC-234: Every item of the tab menu and the tree menu whose action has a key shows that key (Close and Close All, Rename, Delete, Find File References), and the two menus call the same action by the same name.
- [x] ISC-235: Anti: a tooltip, an editor hint or the delete dialog never splits a word that fits on a line by itself; the whole word moves to the next line, and only a word longer than the line (a path) is cut.
- [x] ISC-236: Anti: the status bar never shows the operating system name; it holds a message or the find match count and nothing else (the shown file's path or name is in the title bar).
- [x] ISC-237: The key shown at the right of a menu item sits on the same text baseline as the item label, with the font sizes unchanged (label 14 px, key 12 px).
- [x] ISC-238: The frontmatter pill says only "Frontmatter"; it never lists the block's field names.
- [x] ISC-239: While Ctrl (Cmd) is held and the pointer is over a link that Ctrl+click follows, the cursor is a pointer; over plain text, a missing link, a table-cell link or with the keys let go, it is not.
- [x] ISC-240: While a tree row is dragged the cursor is a closed hand over a folder it may move into and a not-allowed sign everywhere else; the normal cursor is back after a drop or Escape.
- [x] ISC-241: Pressing a resize handle, the sidebar edge or a split handle between panes, gives the handle keyboard focus, so the Left and Right arrow keys resize it and never move the editor caret.
- [x] ISC-242: The browser's own right-click menu (the one with "Inspect") never shows anywhere in the app except on editor text and text boxes; the tab menu and the tree menu still open. (The exception for text was removed on 2026-11-11 by ISC-251.)
- [x] ISC-243: Copy Path and Copy Relative Path write the same kind of separator: backslashes for a Windows path, forward slashes for any other; the relative path never mixes the two.
- [x] ISC-244: No error shown to the user contains an OS error code; saving to a file the OS marks read-only says the file is read-only and points to Save As, and other I/O failures are worded in plain sentences.
- [x] ISC-245: A tab opened for the first time in a pane starts at its caret (the top for a new file), whatever the tab before it was scrolled to; a tab that was shown before comes back to the place it was left.
- [x] ISC-246: A file the user picks by hand (a tree click, Quick Open, the open dialog) gets a tab in the active pane even when another pane shows it, sharing one document, text and undo; links, history, references and recent files still jump to the tab that shows the file.
- [x] ISC-247: A pane's tab strip scrolls sideways with no visible scrollbar, its tabs never shrink below 96 px, the active tab is kept in view, and two buttons stay fixed at its right end: + makes a new file in that pane and a chevron lists the pane's tabs.
- [x] ISC-248: Every scrolling area (editor, sidebar tree, palette and menu lists, panels) shows a thin, rounded, themed scrollbar with no arrow buttons, in both themes; the tab strip shows none.
- [ ] ISC-249: A setting (and a palette command) turns the F12 developer tools on or off while the app runs; a release build has them off until the setting turns them on.
- [x] ISC-250: A tab with unsaved changes shows its dot centred exactly where the close button's X appears, in the same 20 px slot, and the tab's label does not change width when the pointer enters the tab.
- [x] ISC-251: The browser's right-click menu (Inspect, Cut, Copy, Paste) never appears anywhere in the app, editor text and text boxes included; the tab menu and the file tree menu still open.
- [x] ISC-252: Renaming `A/a.md` to `A/b.md` rewrites only the links that lead to `A/a.md`: a link to `B/a.md` is left alone, whether it sits in a file at the root, in `A` or in `B`.
- [x] ISC-253: The scans behind rename, move and References read only Markdown files (md, markdown, mdown, mkd, mdx, any case) and never enter a `node_modules` folder (since Phase 7.1, none of the built-in excluded folders of ISC-282); Quick Open lists every file outside those folders.
- [x] ISC-254: With the sidebar tree focused: Up/Down move the selected row without opening it, Right expands a folder or steps into its first child, Left collapses or steps to the parent, Home and End go to the first and last row, Enter toggles a folder or opens a file and moves focus into it, Space opens a file and keeps focus in the tree; a modified arrow (Alt, Ctrl, Meta) is left to the global shortcuts.
- [x] ISC-255: A single click on a file in the tree opens it and leaves focus on its row (no caret in the editor); a double click opens it and puts the caret in the editor; a double click on a folder toggles it once; Quick Open, the open dialog, links, history, references and tab clicks still move focus into the editor.
- [x] ISC-256: Focus stays in the tree after a rename, a delete and a drag-move; the selected row is drawn strong while the tree has focus and dimmer when it does not, with no outline ring.
- [x] ISC-257: A "Focus Sidebar" command, on Ctrl+Shift+E and in the command palette, shows the sidebar if it is hidden and moves focus to the file tree, from anywhere.
- [x] ISC-258: One Ctrl+Z after Replace or Replace all gives the text back in the document and in every pane that shows it; Replace all is a single undo step, not one per match.
- [x] ISC-259: In a read-only file the Replace and Replace all buttons are off and the Enter key in the replace box changes nothing; Find still marks and steps through matches.
- [x] ISC-260: Find in a 10 MB plain-source file reaches its last match; the match count stops at 10,000 matches or after 20 ms (checked every 128 KB of text and every 128 matches) and then reads "N+", or "Count stopped" when it found none; marks are drawn for the viewport only (at most 1,500), so one-letter queries cost a bounded time.
- [x] ISC-261: Find Next onto a match inside text that live preview hides (a link target, a table cell, frontmatter, an image source) selects the match and shows the source of that element; with the selection elsewhere it is drawn again.
- [x] ISC-262: Ctrl+F, Ctrl+H, F3 and Shift+F3 are registry commands (Find, Replace, Find Next, Find Previous) and appear in the app menu; the app takes the keys with no file open so the browser find never opens; CodeMirror's own search keys are not installed (no Ctrl+D or Ctrl+Shift+L multiple selections).
- [x] ISC-263: Escape in either box and the close button close the find bar, remove the marks and put the caret in the editor.
- [x] ISC-264: The find bar belongs to a pane: it stays when another tab is shown there and searches that tab; the query comes off the tab that is left and off every tab when the bar closes; the query text and switches are shared by all panes.
- [x] ISC-265: Whole word matching in Find: a match with a letter or digit (any script) on either side is skipped when the switch is on, also with regular expressions; folder search gets the same switch in P7-T2.
- [x] ISC-266: Folder search reads every text file in every open folder exactly once, including dot-folders, and never enters `.git` or `node_modules`, follows links, or reads a binary file (a NUL, even late in text that decodes as UTF-8); a file over 32 MiB and text in an encoding mdmate does not write are counted as not read, and the list says how many.
- [x] ISC-267: A folder search result carries the line number and the UTF-16 column the editor uses (LF, CRLF and a lone CR end a line, a byte order mark is not text, UTF-16 files are read, an emoji counts as two units), and opening a result puts the caret on the start of the match.
- [x] ISC-268: Plain text, Match case, Whole word and patterns mean the same in folder search as in Find; a table of 28 queries runs through both engines and the two known differences (`\w` and `\d` read Unicode in Rust only) are rows of their own; a pattern with look-around or a back-reference gets a plain message and no results.
- [x] ISC-269: Folder search reads an open document with unsaved edits from the text the page sends, so its lines and columns are the ones on screen and text that was deleted is not found; a document with no unsaved edits is read from the file.
- [x] ISC-270: The result list draws only the rows on screen (under 60 for 6,000 results), follows the scroll, brings the selected result into view, and a search that reaches 10,000 results stops there and says only the first 10,000 are listed.
- [x] ISC-271: Results behave like the file tree: a click shows the file at the match in the active pane with focus staying in the list, Enter and a double click put the caret in the file, the arrow keys, Home, End and the page keys move the selection and open nothing, keys with Ctrl, Alt or Meta pass to the app, Down in the box enters the list and Up from the first result returns to the box, Escape in the box stops a running search or returns to the editor, and no native context menu appears.
- [x] ISC-272: The sidebar header always shows Folders and Search (and References when there is a list); the Search panel loads on first use; Search in Folders (Ctrl+Shift+F, in the app menu) shows it with the caret in the box and the selected line of the editor as the query; Enter searches and typing does not; changing an option runs the search again only once one was made.
- [x] ISC-273: Starting a search stops the one running and drops its late batches and answer; Escape or the Stop button stops one within half a second and the list says "Search stopped" with what was found; a failed search is a plain message with no results; the list says how many files were not read.
- [x] ISC-274: The session journal writes one second after the last change, and no later than five seconds after the first change when changes do not pause, so typing in a 1 MB file with unsaved edits adds one snapshot every five seconds and not one a second, a lone edit is on disk one second after it, and a force-kill loses at most five seconds of typing without a pause.
- [x] ISC-275: The outline lists ATX headings (up to three spaces of indent, closing hashes dropped) and setext headings (a `=` or `-` bar under a paragraph line), each with its level and the line of its text, and lists none from fenced code (an unclosed fence hides the rest), front matter (`---` on line 1 closed by `---` or `...` within 200 lines), lists, quotes or indented code; LF, CRLF and a lone CR end a line.
- [x] ISC-276: The Rust scan, the editor's own scan (which also numbers the `#anchor` links) and the browser mock agree on a table of 32 cases that Rust and vitest both run, and a list stops at 50,000 headings and says only the first 50,000 are listed.
- [x] ISC-277: Outline rows behave like the file tree: a click shows the heading in the active pane's tab with focus staying in the list, Enter and a double click put the caret in the file, the arrow keys, Home, End and the page keys move the selection and the caret stays, keys with Ctrl, Alt, Meta or Shift pass to the app, only the rows on screen are drawn (under 60 for 5,000 headings), and a selection past the end of a shorter list is dropped.
- [x] ISC-278: The sidebar header always shows Folders, Search and Outline; the Outline panel loads on first use; Show Outline (Ctrl+Shift+L, in the app menu) shows it with focus in the list; it says in plain words when nothing is open, when the file is not Markdown, when there are no headings, while it reads and when it could not; rows read as drawn (bold and link markup gone, an empty heading as "(empty heading)") and are indented by level.
- [x] ISC-279: The outline scans only while its panel is on screen: at once when the tab or document changes, 400 ms after typing pauses for a change of text (other notifications after an edit do not push the wait back), and never for a caret move or a save that leaves the text as it was; a document with no unsaved edits is read from its file by path and one with unsaved edits or no file from its text; an answer that is late, replaced by a newer scan or arrives after the panel went is not shown.
- [x] ISC-280: Anti: the Outline panel never pushes typing past 16 ms: p95 work per key in a 1 MB file with the panel open and the document edited every 700 ms stays under 16 ms, in dispatch and trusted mode, and repeats.
- [x] ISC-295: The three toggles (Match case, Whole word, Regular expression) sit inside the folder search box at its right, with larger icons and the Find bar's look: a filled primary button when on, flat when off.
- [x] ISC-296: "files to include" and "files to exclude" sit behind a `...` toggle under the search box, closed by default, and open when either has text or Find in Folder fills one; Enter in either runs the search.
- [x] ISC-297: The two fields take VS Code's patterns: comma-separated (a comma inside `{}` does not split), `*` `?` `**` `{a,b}` `[abc]` `[!abc]`, a backslash read as `/`, case-insensitive on Windows; a bare pattern matches at any depth and covers what is inside a folder it names; `./p` anchors to the top of a root; no `!`.
- [x] ISC-298: Include narrows the search and exclude removes from it; a folder exclude names is not entered; a file the filters drop is never opened or counted; unsaved text is filtered like files; `.git` and `node_modules` stay skipped whatever the patterns say.
- [x] ISC-299: A malformed pattern marks its field, the summary names the field and the reason, and no search runs.
- [x] ISC-300: Find in Folder (the context menu on a folder or root row, and Alt+Shift+F with the tree focused on one) shows the Search panel and fills "files to include" with `./relative/path` (`./RootName/relative/path` with several roots, `./RootName` for a root row, the full path when two roots share a name, `* ? [ ] { }` escaped), leaving the query and "files to exclude" alone and not running the search; Ctrl+Shift+F never changes the include box.
- [x] ISC-301: With several or nested roots, `./RootName/...` is matched against the root with that name and an unknown name is an error; a file under two roots matches when it matches under any root that holds it.
- [x] ISC-302: The Stop button is outlined and a thin moving bar shows under the search header while a search runs; Stop ends the search within half a second, keeps what was found, and both are gone when it ends.
- [x] ISC-303: Anti: typing in "files to include" or "files to exclude" never starts a search; Enter or a toggle does.
- [x] ISC-304: A pattern in "files to include" or "files to exclude" never limits a search unseen: while the two boxes are folded away, the `...` button carries a dot whenever either has text, and the dot goes when both are empty or the boxes are open.
- [x] ISC-305: The result line names where the search looked (`in ./docs`, `excluding *.txt`, both joined by a comma) from what that search ran with, not from what is typed now; before a search it shows where the next one would look.
- [x] ISC-306: A search result that opens a file marks the matched words in the editor in the Find bar's current-match color, whether focus stays in the list (click) or moves to the file (Enter, double click), in both themes; the mark goes away on the next edit or move of the caret and never outlives it.
- [x] ISC-307: Close All, from a tab menu or the Close All command, closes the tabs of one pane only (the pane the menu was opened in, or the focused pane for the command), asking once for each dirty document; that pane goes away unless it is the last one; the other panes keep their tabs. A document still shown by a tab in another pane stays open and is not asked about.
- [x] ISC-308: Close to the Right, in the tab menu, closes every tab to the right of the one that was right-clicked, in that pane only, asking once for each dirty document; it is off on the last tab of a pane.
- [x] ISC-309: Every hit the folder search found in a file is marked in the text of the editor showing the file, in the muted Find match color (the one a click opened stays in the stronger current-match color), and along its scrollbar as a small tick behind a semi-transparent thumb; the ticks move with the text when it is edited and are gone when the search is cleared, replaced or has no hit in that file.
- [x] ISC-310: The Find bar's query marks every place it matches along the scrollbar the same way, whatever the size of the file (it gives up after 10,000 matches or 30 ms and draws what it found), and the ticks go when the bar is closed or the query is empty.

### F13 · Workspace index
Why: a large workspace stays quick to search only when mdmate keeps a list of its files instead of walking the disk for every request.

- [x] ISC-281: The workspace index lists every file under the roots with its path, name, kind, size and modified time, and builds in the background when a root opens; file contents are not indexed.
- [x] ISC-282: The index skips a built-in list of folder names wherever they appear (`.git`, `node_modules`, `target`, `dist`, `build`, `out`, `coverage`, `.next`, `.nuxt`, `.svelte-kit`, `.turbo`, `.cache`, `.parcel-cache`, `.venv`, `venv`, `__pycache__`, `.gradle`, `bin`, `obj`, `.vs`, `.idea`) and never reads `.gitignore`; the tree still shows those folders and their files open from there.
- [x] ISC-283: The index is cached on disk, one versioned file per root in the app data folder, holding paths and metadata only; it is rebuilt when its version, the exclusion list or the root changes or when it cannot be read, and deleted when the root is removed.
- [x] ISC-284: At start, Ctrl+P answers from the cached index at once while a reconcile against the disk runs in the background, and its results update when the reconcile ends.
- [x] ISC-285: mdmate's own new file or folder, rename, move, delete and copy on drop update the index and its cache at once.
- [x] ISC-286: Changes made by other programs reach the index through a recursive watcher per root that ignores excluded folders; when events are dropped, that root is reconciled again.
- [x] ISC-287: A file under two nested roots appears once in the index, relative to the innermost root that holds it.
- [x] ISC-288: The index holds at most 1,000,000 files across all roots; past that the status bar names the root that was cut, and search and link scans walk that root.
- [x] ISC-289: While a root is indexed or reconciled, the status bar shows an Indexing label and a thin percent bar: folders checked out of the cached folders when there is a cache, and top-level entries done on a first index.
- [x] ISC-290: Quick Open answers from the index and never walks the disk when the picker opens.
- [x] ISC-291: Ctrl+P answers in under 50 ms p95 on a 100,000-file workspace.
- [x] ISC-292: Anti: indexing a root never pushes typing past 16 ms p95 work per key.
- [x] ISC-293: The rename and move rewrite, Find File References and folder search take their file list from the index, and walk only a root that is not indexed yet or was cut by the cap.
- [x] ISC-294: Quick Open, link scans and folder search offer no file under an excluded folder.
- [x] ISC-313: Ctrl+P matches a query with no slash against file names only, so a folder name never makes a file match; a query with a slash (or a backslash) matches the whole path.
- [x] ISC-314: Ctrl+P lists only the files whose name holds the typed text as one unbroken run (case and spaces aside); when no file does, it lists files whose name holds the letters in order, so `pln` still finds `plan.md`.
- [x] ISC-315: With `links.onRename` set to `never` (the default until Phase 8 says otherwise), a rename or move only renames or moves, when it can be done: nothing is asked, no file is read for links, and no link is changed.
- [x] ISC-316: With `links.onRename` set to `ask`, a rename or move asks before any file is read for links; the buttons are "Yes" (first, has the keyboard), "No" and "Cancel"; only "Yes" reads the workspace and then rewrites with no second question, "No" only renames or moves, and Cancel leaves everything as it was.
- [x] ISC-317: A rename or move that cannot be done (a name that is taken or not allowed) is refused where it was typed before anything is asked or read, and one that would move nothing (the same name, the same folder) asks nothing.
- [x] ISC-318: After links were updated a line says how many links in how many files (or that no link pointed to the file); a line with nothing wrong in it goes away by itself and leaves the keyboard where it was, and one that lists files that kept their links or could not be read stays until dismissed and takes the keyboard.
- [x] ISC-319: The `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` environment variable (`ask`, `update` or `never`, any case, spaces ignored) sets `links.onRename` while `settings.json` does not exist, is read when the app runs, and wins over the setting; unset, empty or unknown leaves the default, `never`.
- [ ] ISC-320: `settings.json` holds `links.onRename` (`ask`, `update` or `never`) and a saved change applies to the next rename with no restart; what `MDMATE_LINKS_ON_RENAME` does once the file exists is decided and written in the Phase 8 decisions.
- [x] ISC-321: A rename or move reads files for links only in the workspace roots that hold the file (all of them when roots lie inside one another), so another workspace is neither read nor changed; Find File References looks in the same roots, and in every root for a file no root holds.

## Decisions

- 2026-11-07: Chose CodeMirror 6 over ProseMirror-family editors, Lexical and Monaco. Text stays the document, which gives byte-exact saves by construction, and Obsidian's editor runs on it. No head-to-head benchmark exists, so P0 measures it.
- 2026-11-07: Started from scratch instead of reusing MarkdownWeave. The owner chose this for performance; only its lessons carry over, not its code.
- 2026-11-07: Chose CM6 nested parsers over Shiki, so code blocks stay editable text with token colors and no worker.
- 2026-11-07: Chose hot exit over autosave and close prompts, matching SublimeText.
- 2026-11-07: Chose one headless master state per document with follower views, so undo history outlives any pane.
- 2026-11-07: Stored resized images as `<img src width>`, since that renders on GitHub while `=WxH` and `|300` do not.
- 2026-11-07: Chose pointer-based dragging, because `dragDropEnabled: false` would lose the real file paths on native drops.
- 2026-11-07: Dropped `panic = abort`, so a crashed background thread can't take down the app and lose unsaved typing.
- 2026-11-07: Link rewrite scans every text file in the workspace, including folders hidden from the tree. Only `.git` and binary files are skipped. (Replaced on 2026-11-11 by ISC-253: Markdown files only, and not inside `node_modules`.)
- 2026-11-07: Structural probes run through `scripts/check-arch.mjs`, since regex pipes would break the Test Strategy table.
- 2026-11-07: refined: ISC-51 split into ISC-51.1, ISC-51.2 and ISC-51.3, since one claim bundled three probes.
- 2026-11-07: refined: added ISC-169 and ISC-170, since building the task list showed LICENSE and README had no claims.
- 2026-11-07: refined: ISC-12 widened from `invoke` to all `@tauri-apps` imports, so the browser mock stays complete.
- 2026-11-07: refined: ISC-47 moved from a manual run to a scripted kill and restore.
- 2026-11-07: refined: added ISC-171 to ISC-176, since phase plans held behaviors no claim covered.
- 2026-11-07: refined: added ISC-177 to ISC-179 for drag-to-move of selected text, which the owner put back in v1.
- 2026-11-07: refined: ISC-7 is judged on the main-thread work a key causes (handling it, plus the next frame's style, layout and paint). Key to frame adds an idle wait of up to one frame, which no editor can remove, so it is printed and never judged.
- 2026-11-07: Kill rule checked in P0-T8, and it did not fire. Throwaway live-preview decorations hold typing at 5.0 ms p95 per key (5.5 ms with real key events) on the 1 MB fixture, against 16 ms. They cover inline marks only; Phase 3 measures the real layer, block widgets included, with the same scenarios.
- 2026-11-07: Chose 3 MB as the plain-source cutoff. The Markdown parser is what slows typing (p99 8.2 ms at 3 MB and 15 ms at 5 MB, against 16), and plain source types at 1.9 ms even at 10 MB. Provisional until Phase 3 measures the real live-preview layer.
- 2026-11-08: Confirmed the 3 MiB cutoff with the real live-preview layer (P3-T6). Typing p95 / p99 in ms: 2 MB 6.0 / 6.9, 3 MB 8.2 / 9.0, 5 MB 12.6 / 13.5, 10 MB 16.5 / 17.4 (over 16, not repeatable). The 3 MB fixture is 3,146,134 bytes, 406 over the 3 MiB cutoff, so that row is the largest size that gets the parser, with p99 9.0 against 16; the number stays. No longer provisional.
- 2026-11-08: The owner allowed raising the ISC-63 limit if repeat runs agreed, and I set it from 20 to 35 percent (the worst clean run was 1.28), and the owner confirmed the number. The full layer missed 20 in four of six runs (late-file ratio 1.28, 1.27, 1.20, 1.20, 1.16, plus one noisy run at 1.05). A scan late in a 10 MB file takes about 48 microseconds against 40 in a 1 MB file, the cost of finding a place in a tree ten times as big; a whole view update stays level (0.42 against 0.41 ms). The run with the new limit: worst deviation 14.7 percent, control 8.5 times. The control that grows with the file is what stops the check passing a real slope.
- 2026-11-08: Raised ISC-4 from 1000 ms to 1500 ms, on the owner's call (the fallback named on 2026-11-07). The real shell measured 515 ms warm and 693, 893 and 804 ms on three first launches with a fresh WebView2 profile (P3-T6), so 1000 ms passed with a worst margin of 107 ms; the owner wants room for a slower machine or a first launch after a reboot. Past 1500 ms the number stops catching regressions, so it stays there.
- 2026-11-07: Chose normalized text with the line endings kept beside it, over keeping the carriage return in the text. The second keeps every byte, but the Markdown parser then misses tables and hard breaks.
- 2026-11-07: Raised ISC-4 from 500 ms to 1000 ms, on the owner's call. A bare shell with an editor is editable in 429 ms on a warm profile and 475 ms on a first launch with a fresh profile, mostly WebView2 starting, so 1000 ms leaves room for the real UI. Phase 3 measures it, and 1500 ms is the fallback.
- 2026-11-07: Chose the asset protocol with a scope that grows at runtime for images, over a custom protocol. Tested in the real WebView2: a folder is blocked until it is allowed, then loads, including names with spaces and accents.
- 2026-11-07: Kept every strict TypeScript flag and mark generated shadcn-vue files with ts-nocheck and a reason, since all 69 type errors come from exactOptionalPropertyTypes in generated code. Never run shadcn-vue init here, because it rewrites tokens.css.
- 2026-11-07: Kept drag-to-move for selected text in v1, on the owner's call. CM6's own text drag does not work while native file drops are on (tested by hand), and the file paths from native drops matter more, so it is built on pointer events like tab and tree dragging (ISC-177 to ISC-179).
- 2026-11-07: Drag-to-move spike passed in the real WebView2: 15 unit tests, 11 of 11 trusted-input cases, and the owner's 12-step by-hand sitting, with a real file drop working while it was active and no browser drag event ever firing. ISC-177 to ISC-179 stay open until P5-T7 builds it into the app. The spike did not cover 125% display scale, line endings in moved text, or live-preview widgets.
- 2026-11-07: Tauri drop positions are physical pixels (tested at 125% scale), so the platform layer divides them by the pixel ratio before anything hit-tests.
- 2026-11-07: Shipping custom caption buttons without the Windows 11 snap layout hover flyout, which needs the OS to see a real caption button. Win+Z and edge snapping work on the undecorated window (tested by hand).
- 2026-11-07: refined: the probe for ISC-14 now also catches Tailwind palette classes (`bg-white`, `text-slate-500`), since they are colors no theme file can change. Named CSS colors stay unchecked, because the words are too common in prose and identifiers.
- 2026-11-07: Chose two original built-in themes, Graphite (dark, `#1d2027` base) and Paper (light), with every color in `src/styles/themes/` and a test for token parity, gamut and contrast. The native window color is the dark background, pinned to `tauri.conf.json` by a test.
- 2026-11-07: refined: ISC-26 and ISC-27 moved from `manual` to `screenshot`. Both can be driven and read by tools on the real window: trusted clicks with the OS window state read back, and a 60 fps screen recording scanned frame by frame.
- 2026-11-07: Launch frames are checked at 60 fps (frames 17 ms apart), so a white flash shorter than about two frames can slip through. A control window that was white for about 100 ms was caught; one that lived 43 ms was not.
- 2026-11-07: Startup with the full shell is 435 ms median on a warm profile (5 runs, every run within 10%), against 1000 ms. The bare shell took 429 to 475 ms, so the title bar, tab strip, sidebar frame and status bar cost almost nothing.
- 2026-11-07: Closing the last tab of the only pane leaves that pane empty, instead of removing it. ISC-31 holds for every pane that has a sibling; removing the last pane would leave no workbench to open a file into.
- 2026-11-07: Split sizes are percentages that always add up to 100, and every split node carries an id so a view can key it and a resize can name it. Moving a tab to the pane it is already in, and dropping the only tab of a pane on that pane's own edge, change nothing beyond order.
- 2026-11-07: Chose Rust for the whole byte transform (decode, split off the line endings, join, encode) and a raw frame of header, ending exceptions and newline-only text over IPC in both directions. The ISC-1 and ISC-2 probes are Rust tests, so the code that touches bytes has to be Rust; the page owns the text and the ending of each break.
- 2026-11-07: Chose `rfd` directly for the open dialog over `tauri-plugin-dialog`, since the plugin pulls `tauri-plugin-fs` into the build graph and ISC-21 says that crate is absent.
- 2026-11-07: Chose a path policy where the page can read and save only files Rust saw the user choose, or files inside an open workspace folder, compared with symlinks resolved. The page has no command that grants access. Saves go by a Rust-issued document id, never by a path.
- 2026-11-07: refined: the P0 decision (newline-only text, endings in a state field) supersedes the Phase 2 plan line "Set `EditorState.lineSeparator` from the file" and the matching architecture bullet. `lineSeparator` stays at its default.
- 2026-11-07: 2026-11-07: Kept open documents in a plain map with a small reactive record for the chrome, and made closing the last tab of an edited document ask first, in a banner inside the pane instead of a modal. Why: rule one keeps editor objects out of Vue, and closing the app never prompts because hot exit keeps the edits (ISC-49). Easy to change if the owner prefers a modal.
- 2026-11-07: 2026-11-07: Gave each tab a follower state and kept undo history on one headless master per document. Parked tabs only note changes and apply them as one change when shown. Why: closing a pane never loses history (ISC-43), and typing in one pane does not make every parked tab parse. Selection undo (Mod-u) is not bound on followers.
- 2026-11-07: 2026-11-07: Recognised a move by the shape of its change, one deletion and one insertion of the same text, instead of an effect carried from the follower. Why: the master receives only the change set, the undo and redo of a move have the same shape, and moving text is a move whoever does it. Two cursors that delete and type the same newlines look like a move too, which only changes the endings of the breaks typed there.
- 2026-11-07: Decided what counts as an outside change by content, not by event: Rust keeps the hash each document last read or wrote and reports only a different one. Why: one save raises several events, notify can lose some, and the app's own write must be recognised; one comparison handles all three. A save records its hash before it writes, because the watcher thread can see the new file before the save returns.
- 2026-11-07: Made opening a file that is already open leave Rust's record alone, and the page treats a different hash as an outside change. Why: refreshing the record made the host believe the page was current while it still held the old text, which would let a save overwrite a change the page never saw.
- 2026-11-07: Hot exit writes `session.json` (layout, tabs, carets, unsaved buffers) one journal interval after a change and once on close; the interval is 1000 ms. Why: a write costs the page about one late frame at 1 MB (5 ms to build, 26 ms to confirm) and two at 4 MB, so 1000 ms means about 1 late frame in 60 while typing (worked out, not measured with the journal live) and at most a second of typing lost to a force-kill. Measured in P2-T6 (`pnpm perf journal-cost`); easy to change in `JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS` if the owner wants fewer writes or less to lose.
- 2026-11-08: Link targets that run code are refused outright, on the owner's call (P5-T1): programs, scripts, shortcuts, installers and mountable images (`.exe`, `.bat`, `.ps1`, `.hta`, `.lnk`, `.url`, `.msi`, `.iso` and the rest of the list in `src-tauri/src/links/guard.rs`), judged on the resolved name. Why: under "ask first" a single yes would run code from a document. ISC-18 still holds, since such a file never opens at all.
- 2026-11-08: `file:` URLs are refused like any other scheme, host-less `file:///C:/...` included, on the owner's call (P5-T1). Links are relative paths, anchors, or `http`, `https` and `mailto`.
- 2026-11-08: Following a Markdown link lets the page open that one file, `..` included, on the owner's call (P5-T1, used by P5-T2). Rust resolves the target against its own record of the linking document; the page sends the document id and the target as written and still has no command that names a path to grant.
- 2026-11-07: Applied the P0 cutoff: files above 3 MiB open as plain source with a notice, decided once when the document opens by the size on disk. Why: the parser is what makes typing slow at size (P0-T9), and the same rule covers restored sessions. Known limit: a file that grows past the cutoff while open keeps the parser until reopened.
- 2026-11-09: refined: added ISC-190 and ISC-191 on the owner's call (wording agreed), since P5-T6 and P5-T7 built behavior no claim covered: lang-markdown's own paste handler changed read-only documents, and a drop on a drawn block would merge text into its source.
- 2026-11-09: Tab outside a list stays in the editor on the owner's answer: Tab inserts a tab (or indents the selection), Shift+Tab outdents, and the indent unit is a tab. Before, Tab outside a list moved focus out of the editor.
- 2026-11-09: refined: the decorations-scale and reveal scenarios add the content's top padding when they check that a line is in view (`settleOnLine`, owner-approved). Line tops count from below the padding P4-T5 added, so a line in view read 16 px above the top and the scenario never settled. Limit, documents, control and same-work check unchanged.
- 2026-11-09: refined: added ISC-192 to ISC-218 (F12 · File lifecycle) on the owner's call after a grilling session: Open and Remove Folder, drop from Explorer (open, copy into a tree folder, folder as root), New Folder, untitled files, Save As, Revert, Close All, Open Recent, a tab context menu and Find File References. Building Phase 5 showed no phase opened a dropped file, created a file outside a folder, or added a root at all. Planned in Phase 6 (P6-T1, T2, T3 extended; P6-T8, T9 added).
- 2026-11-09: Rust keeps the workspace roots in `workspace.json` in the data folder (written atomically on each add or remove, read and granted again at start); the session snapshot holds only which folders were open. Why: a file the page writes must never be able to grant access, and a snapshot is exactly that. A damaged or missing session still brings the roots back (P6-T1).
- 2026-11-09: Removing a root revokes Rust's access to it at once but not the image protocol's scope for it, which lasts until restart. Why: Tauri's asset scope can only grow, and `forbid_directory` is permanent and beats later allows, which would block adding the same folder again. Images in a removed folder can still be shown until the app restarts (P6-T1).
- 2026-11-09: The tree lists one folder when it opens (lazily, again at every open) and Rust watches exactly the open folders, not recursively; an event names the folder to list again. Why: a workspace can hold far more files than the sidebar shows, and a recursive watch of every root costs handles and events for rows nobody sees (P6-T1).
- 2026-11-09: Rust records the paths of each native drop from Tauri's drop event (the last four drops) and `drop_describe` and `add_dropped_root` accept only those; the page never names a path. Why: dropped files can come from anywhere, and the page must not be able to ask for a path it was not given (P6-T1).
- 2026-11-09: refined: added ISC-219 (F12 · File lifecycle) before building P6-T2. A rename or move in the tree changes the path of a file Rust holds open by id; without it the next save would write the old path and bring back the file that was just renamed, which breaks "the file on disk belongs to the writer". It covers a folder with open files under it. Implied by ISC-101 and ISC-102, but no claim stated it.

- 2026-11-10: P6-T7 choices: one picker component serves both keys and loads the first time one opens (`defineAsyncComponent`; only `features/palette/state.ts` is in the entry chunk, which the build and the `palette-lazy` arch rule both show). The file index is Rust (`workspace/quick.rs`): it lists every file under every root except `.git` by the same walk the link scan uses, builds on the first query (nothing is read before the first Ctrl+P), is rebuilt in the background each time the picker opens so a file made a moment ago shows up (a second build never starts while one runs), and is cut at 250,000 files with a notice. Matching needs the letters in order, ignores case and the spaces in the query, and ranks a run of letters, a word start and a match in the file name higher; the same points are written twice, in Rust for files and in `src/lib/fuzzy.ts` for command titles and the browser stand-in, and each is tested on its own. Commands that cannot run show greyed and do nothing when chosen; the two commands that open a picker are not listed in it; the same key closes the picker and the other key switches to the other picker; every other shortcut is held off while a picker is open. Closing gives focus back to where it was unless something else took it (opening a file does). Full reasons in `ai-docs/phases/phase-06/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-10: refined: added ISC-220 and ISC-221 (F12 · File lifecycle) on the owner's answers after P6-T9. Rename of a file outside every open folder: Rust could grant the parent folder, but that would let the page read and write every file beside it, so the owner chose to keep it off and explain why in a tooltip on the disabled menu item (no rename field was built for such files; Save As makes a renamed copy). Find File References gets Shift+F12: it is the key VS Code uses for Find All References and has no browser meaning, while Ctrl+Shift+R is the WebView2 hard reload.
- 2026-11-10: refined: tooltips are always the shadcn Tooltip, themed with the menu tokens (`bg-popover`), never the native `title` attribute (owner call). ISC-220 now says so. The `title` on the Rename wrapper became a Tooltip around it. Eight native `title` sites that exist in the app (tab titles, tree rows, the title-bar menu, the rewrite banner, the References panel) are still to convert; they are listed in the Phase 6 decisions.
- 2026-11-10: refined: added ISC-222 (F12 · File lifecycle) after the owner said to convert every native tooltip, not only the Rename one. One small wrapper, `HoverHint`, holds the shadcn Tooltip so the 8 sites stay short; a test scans the `.vue` files so a new native `title` fails the build. Markdown image titles drawn inside the document are document content, not app tooltips, and stay.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-223 to ISC-233 (F12 · File lifecycle) from the owner's walk through the Phase 6 UAT, after a grilling round in which the owner agreed every answer: a right click on a tab always opens the menu (not reproduced at HEAD in the debug window, with or without the tooltip showing; the tooltip moves off the tab root onto the label anyway); tab hints only when the name is cut off, 1 s for tabs and tree rows; the arrow never covers the box; editor tooltips (broken link, frontmatter pill, failed image) are themed, and the scan covers `.ts`; Delete asks first and then closes the tabs of what it deletes, unsaved ones too, which reverses "Delete asks nothing" and amends ISC-103; the tree menu gets Copy Path, Copy Relative Path, Find File References and Open Folder; the sidebar resizes and remembers its width; an image may climb as far as its workspace folder (a decision Phase 4 left open); an image tab keeps its zoom.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-234 after the owner noticed the tab menu showed F2 for Rename and the tree menu did not, while both showed Shift+F12. Option A of the review: every item whose action has a key shows it, read from the command registry where the key is a command (Ctrl+W, Ctrl+Shift+W, F2, Shift+F12) and from the tree's own constant for the Delete key it handles itself; Rename reads "Rename..." in both. Known and accepted: Shift+F12, F2 and Ctrl+W act on the active tab, so a hint on a right-clicked background tab, or on a tree row other than the active tab's file, names the key that works on the active tab; dropping those hints was option B and was not chosen.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-235 after the owner saw "load" cut as "loa" / "d" in the image tooltip. The hint box, the editor hint and the delete dialog title used break-all (cut anywhere); they now use overflow-wrap: break-word, which moves the word down first and cuts only a word longer than the line. Known: a path with no spaces still breaks inside a segment (e.g. "wit" / "h/no"), since the browser offers no break after a slash; adding break points after slashes is a possible follow-up.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T9 choices: every tab-menu function takes the tab that was right-clicked, never the active one, and the menu does not activate that tab (the pane still takes focus when the pointer goes down). Close on a background tab with unsaved edits goes through the same question as a keyboard close, which selects that tab so the banner is seen; Close Others closes the other tabs of that one pane through `closeTabs` (the call Close All uses) and stops on Cancel. Copy Path copies the path as the tab holds it (native separators on Windows) through `navigator.clipboard` (no plugin was needed: the real clipboard held the text); Copy Relative Path is the path from the innermost open folder that holds the file with `/`, else the full path. Reveal in Sidebar and Rename need the file to be inside an open folder (the tree has no row for another file), so both are off outside every folder; Rename on an untitled tab is Save As, and F2 runs the same function for the active tab. The menu closes without giving focus back to the tab when the tree's name box is open (`@close-auto-focus`): found in the real window, where the focus return blurred the box and cancelled the rename, which no page test could show. Find File References is one Rust command (`find_references`) built on the rename's own link resolution (`Wanted`), so a listed link is exactly one a rename would rewrite (a test compares the two); it reads an open document with unsaved edits from its buffer, lists at most 2000 links (each line cut at 200 characters) and says when it cut, names the files it could not read, and its list is not refreshed by later edits. The list shows in a References panel in the sidebar (header: Folders / References, with a close button), and `rootOf` now returns the innermost of two nested folders. Full reasons in `ai-docs/phases/phase-06/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T8 choices: an untitled document is an id in Rust and nothing else (a set of ids, no path, no entry in the table of open documents), so it is never watched, never listed and has no file to be recent. The first save writes the file and only then gives the id its path (`promote`); a Save As that is cancelled, refused or fails changes nothing. Save As is one Rust command (`save_as`) that takes the chooser as a parameter, so the dialog is a seam in tests; it refuses a file that another tab has open, refuses to replace the original of a document that cannot be written, treats the document's own file as a plain save (so the disk-changed guard still applies), adds `.md` to a name typed without an extension (and refuses if that file exists), and writes the file's own encoding and BOM when it has them and UTF-8 with no BOM when it has none. The page keeps the same `Document` (and its undo history) and moves it to the new file (`relocate`); the kind of tab and the plain-source choice made at open do not change after Save As. The name offered comes from the first heading (letters and digits only, accents kept, at most 60 characters, fences skipped, `#tag` is not a heading) and the folder from the workspace root that holds the first file tab, else the folder of the last file opened or saved. The recent list is owned by Rust (`recent.json` in the data folder, newest first, 10 files and 5 folders, written atomically, pruned of missing paths each time it is read); only Rust adds to it, and a recent entry is granted again only if it is on the list. Hot exit keeps an untitled tab as a tab with no path plus its buffer, both marked with its number; a restart gives it a new id in Rust, keeps numbers rising during a run, and writes text that has no tab to `recovered/` with a notice. Close All asks once per dirty document, in tab order, and Cancel stops the whole queue (`closeTabs` is the same call Close Others will use in P6-T9). Revert File shows its question in the pane banner; the command is off for an untitled tab and for a file that is gone from disk, and the page looks again when the active document changes, when the window gets focus, when the menu opens and at the moment of the request. Full reasons in `ai-docs/phases/phase-06/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T6 choices: a rename or move runs in two Rust calls, `rewrite_plan` before the move (it scans in the background with progress, validates the move, keeps the plan under a token and returns the counts) and `rewrite_move` after the answer (it takes the plan only with its own token, does the move, then applies); a refused or cancelled prompt leaves nothing changed. The prompt is one banner in the sidebar with Update links, Don't update and Cancel; the scan shows progress and a Stop. An open document is never written to disk by the engine: a clean one is rewritten like a closed file and told to reload, a dirty one gets the same edits as one undoable step in its editor and stays unsaved with the disk copy untouched; the page decides clean or dirty when the answer comes, and a document whose text changed after it was sent is listed as failed, not edited. A dirty document that moves with its folder is read from where it was. `links.confirmRewrite` (default true) is a minimal reactive setting in `src/lib/settings.ts`: there is no settings file until P8-T1, so the user cannot turn the prompt off yet. The browser mock host rewrites inline links only; the real scanner is proven in Rust. Why: the page owns unsaved text and Rust owns paths and files, so each side changes only what it holds; one token per plan stops a late or repeated answer from running an old plan; and keeping the engine off open documents is what stops a rewrite from overwriting the user's typing.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T5 choices: the plan runs before the move and the apply after it, so a refused prompt leaves nothing changed; a link is edited only when, written as it is, it would no longer reach the place it reached before, so a link that is still right keeps its bytes and a folder that moves with its contents rewrites nothing inside; spelling counts in that test (a rename that changes only case rewrites the links) while finding what moved ignores case where the file system does; links are written relative with forward slashes, keeping a leading `./`, a trailing slash and the escaping of the old link; a file that changed since the scan, a read-only file and a file that cannot be written are reported and never overwritten, and the rest still go through; a file in an encoding mdmate does not write is reported at plan time. Why: the rewrite touches files the user did not open, so it must change as little as it can prove it has to.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T4 choices: the plan task builds the scanner, and the claims ISC-116 to ISC-121 and ISC-124 read "rewrite covers X", so T4 also builds the one-link edit step (`Link::render`, `splice`, `apply`) and each claim is proven by scan, edit and exact output; T5 keeps the planning (which links point at the old path, new relative paths, outbound links, folders, atomic writes, failures). A link carries the byte span of the path part only, so a `#fragment`, a title and the wrapping are never inside an edit. Offsets are into the decoded text with line endings intact; the walker decodes a file, the engine splices and encodes with the file's own format, which keeps CRLF, a BOM and UTF-16. Not links: fenced and inline code (a code sample is never rewritten), reference usages, wikilinks, `srcset`, web and mail addresses, anchors, and absolute or network paths. Indented code blocks are not recognised (an indented line is also a list continuation) and a definition must sit on one line. A bad percent escape skips that one link. A file with a NUL byte is binary unless it starts with a UTF-16 mark, so UTF-16 without a mark is skipped as binary; a file in an encoding mdmate does not write is reported, never rewritten. No Markdown crate was added: the scanner is about 600 lines, and a crate gives the range of a whole link, not of its target. Why: the rewrite must never corrupt a document, so every uncertain form is skipped rather than guessed.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T3 choices: a file opens by its extension alone (Markdown, an image extension, or text), and a file that is not UTF-8 opens read-only under the existing notice; a click on any file row opens it; an image dropped on document text does nothing until P9-T2; an image tab keeps no zoom or pan across tab switches; Rust tracks shown images so a restored session grants them again, and `image_closed` removes the grant when the last tab closes; the asset scope for a single image is added when it opens and cannot shrink until restart (the same Tauri limit as for roots); the mock host draws images from `demoImages.ts`; the format keys still work in text tabs, as in large-file plain source; files dropped on blank tree space open in the active pane, and a file already open in another pane opens a second tab in the pane it was dropped on. Why: each keeps untrusted files inert (an SVG is only ever shown through `<img>`) and keeps Rust the only source of paths.
- 2026-11-10: P6-T2 choices: blur on the name box cancels, so a click elsewhere never leaves a half-made file; a new file is selected and revealed but not opened; Delete asked nothing, since the Recycle Bin is the undo (reversed on 2026-11-11, see ISC-227: it asks first now, and closes the tabs of what it deletes); a move to another volume is refused; a folder dropped on a row is copied into that folder, and one dropped on blank tree space still becomes a root; a drop while the clash banner is open is ignored; an unanswered clash counts as Skip; Replace sends the old item to the Recycle Bin before the copy. Why: each keeps the file on disk safe first (the bin, nothing half-written, no surprise folder). Rename and move do not rewrite links yet (P6-T4 to T6), so links to a renamed file are stale until then.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-236 after the owner asked to drop the "windows" word from the status bar corner. The page no longer asks for the platform name there; `host.platformName()` stays because the tree uses it to compare paths.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-237 after the owner saw the menu keys sit higher than their labels. Both texts were centred in the row, so the check "caps centred" read 0 px apart, but the 12 px key's baseline sits about 0.75 px above the 14 px label's baseline, and that is what the eye reads. The key now moves down 0.75 px (`relative top-[0.75px]`, a layout offset, not a transform, so text stays sharp); no font size changed. Applies to the context-menu and dropdown-menu key components and to the title bar submenu detail text.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-238 after the owner asked for the pill to carry just the label. The key list (`frontmatterKeys`, its 48-character cut, the `keys` field of the scan item and widget, the style rule) is removed outright rather than hidden, since nothing else read it. This reverses the Phase 4 decision "the pill label lists the top-level keys"; the keys are one click away in the YAML.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-239 after the owner asked for a pointer cursor on Ctrl+hover over valid links. The same `linkAt` that Ctrl+click uses decides, so the cursor and the click cannot disagree; a link the check found missing (a click only says so) and a link drawn in a table cell (a click does not follow it) keep the text cursor. Ctrl going down or up with the pointer at rest is read from window key events and the last pointer position.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-240 after the owner asked for drag-and-drop cursors in the tree.
