The tree drags with pointer events, not the browser drag and drop (Tauri's file drop would swallow that on Windows), so there is no `dropEffect` to set; the state is one attribute on the root element (`data-tree-drag`) and a rule in `tokens.css` turns it into `cursor: grabbing` or `not-allowed` on everything, `!important`, because the pointer is over elements that set their own cursor. The valid-target test is the same `moveProblem` that decides the drop, so the cursor cannot promise a drop that is refused.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-241 after the owner clicked the sidebar edge and the arrow keys still moved the editor caret. Cause, seen in the real window before any change: the sidebar handle stops the text selection a drag would start with `preventDefault` on pointerdown, which also stops the click from focusing it, and nothing else focused it; the old test sent the key straight to the handle, so it could not see that. The split handles are worse off: reka listens for the press on the whole page, so the press lands on the editor next to the 1 px handle and the handle's own events never fire. The sidebar handle now calls `focus()` itself, and the split handle is focused from reka's `dragging` event, which does fire.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-242 after the owner asked that the WebView menu with "Inspect" never appears. Owner chose "block except text": right-click on editor text and in text boxes keeps the browser menu, since the app has no menu of its own for Cut, Copy and Paste. One capture listener on the document (`src/lib/nativeMenu.ts`, installed in `main.ts` before the app mounts) cancels the default action. It must leave the elements an app menu opens from alone: reka's context menu opens only when nobody has cancelled the event (it checks `defaultPrevented` after a tick), so a blanket cancel would shut the tab menu and the tree menu; the menu cancels the browser one itself when it opens. Found by a test that failed with the first, blanket version, not by reading. Inspect is therefore still reachable by right-clicking editor text; F12 is the setting for Phase 8 (ISC-246).
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-243 after the owner saw Copy Path give a backslash path and Copy Relative Path a forward-slash one on Windows. ISC-215 chose "/" for the relative path without recording a reason (it looks carried over from Markdown link targets, which do use "/"); the owner wants one rule. The relative path now uses the separator the full path is written with (`separatorOf`, shared with `joinPath`): backslashes for a Windows path as Rust gives it, "/" otherwise, and a path that mixes both gets "/". ISC-215 stays closed for what it pinned (root-relative, full path outside every folder, innermost of two roots); only its separator changed.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-244 after the owner saw "Could not save my-title-2.md: Access is denied. (os error 5)" on a read-only file. One mapper in Rust (`friendly_io`, `src-tauri/src/error.rs`) words an I/O failure by its kind first (the same on every OS) and names the few Windows codes that have no kind (32 and 33 in use, 123 and 161 bad name, 206 path too long) through `Platform::os_error_words`, because the architecture check keeps OS-specific code in the platform layer; `AppError::Io` now displays through it, so everything that sent a raw message now sends a clean one. The cases that built their own text from `error.to_string()` (copy, link rewrite, references, open, the trash call) go through it or through `plain_text`, which only strips "(os error N)". A save whose rename is refused on a file with the read-only attribute returns a new kind, `file-read-only`, with the sentence "the file is read-only on disk. Use Save As to keep your changes in a new file". `describe()` on the page strips a leftover code too. A file with the read-only attribute still opens as editable and fails at save; opening it as read-only is a larger change, recorded in untested.md.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-245 after the owner opened a file and found it already scrolled down. Cause, reproduced in the real window before the fix (a file opened after a scrolled one started at 1527 px, and the scrolled one came back at 0): a pane has one CodeMirror view for all its tabs, `view.setState` swaps the text but leaves the scroll container where it was, and nothing saved or restored a tab's place. Each `Follower` now saves `view.scrollSnapshot()` when it leaves the screen and dispatches it when it is shown again. The snapshot names a place in the text, not a pixel count, and is mapped through changes made to the tab while it was parked, so it still means the same line after edits above it. A tab never shown before gets a scroll to its caret (the top for a new file). A jump into the tab (`reveal`) still wins. In memory only: it is not saved in the session file, so a restored tab starts at its caret as before.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-246 and narrowed ISC-174 after the owner asked whether the same file can be open in two panes (it could, only by dragging a tab; a tree click jumped to the other pane's tab). `openInActivePane(path)` in `documents/store.ts` calls `openPath(path, activePane)`, which already focused the tab in that pane or opened one there; the tree click, Quick Open and the open dialog use it. Two tabs on one file are two views of one `Document`: separate caret, selection and scroll, one shared text, one undo history, one dirty state and one save. The routes that mean "go to that file" (link, history step, reference, recent file) keep `openPath` with no pane, so they focus the tab where it is. ISC-174 now says a file is not opened twice within one pane.
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-247 after the owner saw tabs squeeze and a scrollbar on a pane with many tabs. The strip is now a frame (`data-tab-strip`, which the drag code reads as the strip) around a scrolling tab list (`role=tablist`, hidden scrollbar through `scrollbar-width: none` and the WebKit pseudo-element) and a fixed button group. Tabs get `shrink-0` on top of their 96 px minimum, so they scroll instead of squeezing. A native non-passive `wheel` listener turns the mouse wheel into a sideways scroll while the tabs overflow (Vue's `@wheel` would be passive and could not cancel the scroll); a sideways swipe is left to the browser. The active tab is brought into view by moving the strip's own `scrollLeft` (not `scrollIntoView`, which also moves ancestors) when the active tab or the tab count changes. The + calls `newUntitledTab(paneId)`, which takes a pane now, so it works in a pane that is not focused. The chevron opens a shadcn `DropdownMenu` that lists every tab of the pane with a check on the active one and a dot on an unsaved one; picking one selects it and the strip scrolls to it. Both buttons have shadcn tooltips ("New File", "Open Tabs"). The tooltip wraps the whole menu, not its trigger: a tooltip and a menu each anchor to the nearest popper root, and a menu trigger inside a tooltip trigger left the menu parked at the top-left corner with no anchor (found in the real window; jsdom does not show it).
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-248 after the owner found the default Windows scrollbars heavy in the sidebar and content areas. Decided with the owner: plain CSS everywhere, not the shadcn `ScrollArea` component, because that component replaces the scrolling element and the editor's scroller (CodeMirror) and the tree's windowed scroller (ISC-72) must stay the elements they are. One global rule in `tokens.css` styles the WebKit scrollbar pseudo-elements (the WebView is Chromium): 10 px wide with 2 px clear edges so the thumb is 6 px and rounded, a clear track, no arrow buttons, and a thumb that brightens under the pointer. The thumb colors are two new theme tokens (`--scrollbar-thumb`, `--scrollbar-thumb-hover`) with an alpha so they sit on any surface, defined in dark and light and mapped for Tailwind. Nothing may set the standard `scrollbar-width` or `scrollbar-color` (a scan test), because Chromium then ignores the pseudo-element rules for that element; the one exception is `none` on the tab strip, which hides its bar (ISC-247).
- 2026-11-11: refined: added ISC-249 (open, belongs to P8-T1) after the owner asked that F12 be switchable by a future setting. Today F12 opens the WebView developer tools only in a debug build: `wry` turns them on with `cfg(debug_assertions)` and `tauri-runtime-wry` passes the flag through only for `debug_assertions` or the Cargo feature `devtools`, which `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` does not enable (read in the registry sources this session). So a release build already has them off and a debug build has them on; the setting must let the owner turn them on in a release build (that needs the `devtools` feature enabled so the code is compiled in, then a runtime on/off through the window's `open_devtools`/`close_devtools`) and off in a debug one. No code changed in Phase 6.
- 2026-11-11: refined: ISC-72's control changed. The old control, an image widget with no reserved space, drifted 287 to 628 px on earlier builds and 0.4 px on every build tried on 11-11 (HEAD, a890807 and 9412dd5, the commit of the last pass; a fresh profile did not help), so the check failed its own rule that the control must move at least 10 px. A probe showed why: the control's images load during the walk and the page grows by 27000 px, yet CodeMirror keeps the top line still. The control is now the same walk over the real widget with a 40 px jump added to each of 20 steps that the measurement is not told about; it must read at least 10 px back, with no dependence on image timing or display scale. The naive image walk is still run and reported (`naiveImage...`), not gated. The cause of the change on 11-10 to 11-11 is not found.

- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-258 to ISC-265 for P7-T1 (replace as one undo step, read-only files, the 10 MB file, matches inside hidden syntax, the keys, focus on close, the bar per pane, whole word). Whole word came from the owner's review of the Phase 7 report. The find panel is a Vue bar in each pane, not CodeMirror's own panel, because that panel is dropped by every `setState` and cannot hold the app's components; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-266 to ISC-273 for P7-T2 (what folder search reads, lines and columns, the same query meaning as Find, unsaved text, the virtual list and its cap, the keys and focus of the results, the Search tab and its command, stopping and failing). Folder search matches by line in Rust's `regex` with the app's own file walker instead of the `ignore` and `grep-searcher` crates the plan named; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-274 and changed the journal (made before P7-T3 because it showed up in its speed gate). It now writes one interval (1000 ms) after the last change, and at the latest 5 s (`JOURNAL_MAX_WAIT_MS`) after the first change when changes keep coming; before, it wrote one interval after the first change, so typing in a dirty 1 MB file wrote a 1 MB snapshot about once a second. Why: the 2026-11-07 decision above worked out "1 late frame in 60" and said it was not measured with the journal live. P7 measured it: in a 1 MB file the p95 work per key was 14.9 to 16.1 ms with the journal on and 7.2 to 7.5 ms with it off (`typing-outline-open-trusted`, 5 runs each), so the journal put a normal key at the edge of the 16 ms gate. With the new rule the same scenario reads 8.5 ms (7.9 to 8.7), repeatable. Cost: a force-kill during typing that never pauses for a second loses up to 5 s of it (it was 1 s); after any pause of a second nothing is lost beyond the last second, as before. Supersedes "at most a second of typing lost" in the 2026-11-07 line. Easy to change: `JOURNAL_MAX_WAIT_MS` (and `JOURNAL_INTERVAL_MS`) in `src/documents/session.ts`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-275 to ISC-280 for P7-T3 (the scan rules, three scans that agree, the list and its keys, the tab and its messages, when it scans, and the typing anti-claim) and closed ISC-141 to ISC-144. Decisions: the Rust scan is a plain line scan whose result the page turns into display text only for the rows on screen; a heading list is capped at 50,000; the wait after typing is 400 ms and only a change of text restarts it. The typing gate was missed once and led to the journal change (ISC-274), see the decision line above and ISC-280.
- 2026-11-12: Chose a workspace index (Phase 7.1) over honouring `.gitignore` to make large roots fast. Quick Open walked every root each time the picker opened, and link scans and folder search walked on every request. A root-level `.gitignore` would also hide unversioned documentation folders the owner wants searchable, so the index skips a built-in list of build and dependency folder names instead, editable in Phase 8. Settled with the owner: paths and metadata only, a versioned disk cache per root with a background reconcile, updates from mdmate's own operations and a recursive watcher, one entry per file under nested roots (innermost root), a 1,000,000-file cap, and an Indexing label with a percent bar in the status bar. Replaces the `.gitignore` fog line.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-281 to ISC-294 for Phase 7.1 (the index and its exclusions, the cache and the start-up reconcile, updates from mdmate and from the watcher, nested roots, the cap, the status bar, Quick Open and its speed gate, the typing anti-claim, and the link scans and folder search on the index).
- 2026-11-12: refined: ISC-236 now says the status bar holds a message or the find match count, because the owner moved the file path to the title bar (left-aligned, replacing the app name) and the match count into the status bar; the find bar got its switches (regular expression first, larger icons) before the box, Find and Find Prev buttons after it, equal-length boxes, and a more vivid match color.
- 2026-11-12: Chose VS Code's pattern rules for folder search "files to include" and "files to exclude" (globs, not regular expressions), VS Code's Find in Folder entry points and strings, and kept `.git` and `node_modules` as hard skips until Phase 7.1 / 8. Settled with the owner. The Stop button was never seen because it exists only while a search runs and has no fill, so it becomes an outlined button with a progress bar. Task P7-T4; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07/decisions.md`.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-295 to ISC-303 for P7-T4 (toggles in the box, the two fields and their grammar, filters and pruning, bad patterns, Find in Folder, roots, the Stop button, and no search while typing); ISC-273 now names the Stop button.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-304 and ISC-305 after the first run of P7-T4: Ctrl+Shift+F leaves include as it was (as in VS Code), so a leftover pattern limited searches with nothing on screen saying so; the `...` button now carries a dot and the result line names the scope.
- 2026-11-12: refined: added ISC-306 after the first run of P7-T4: a result opened its file with the caret at the start of the match and nothing drawn, and with focus left in the list the editor draws no selection; the match is now marked until the caret moves.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-307 and ISC-308 (P7-T5): Close All now closes only the pane it was asked in (it used to close every tab of every pane), and the tab menu gets Close to the Right, also within its pane (owner call). ISC-211 now says so.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-309 and ISC-310 (P7-T6): the places of the Find query and of the folder search hits show as ticks along the editor scrollbar, as in Sublime Text (owner call). ISC-309 now also marks every hit in the text: after the first run only the clicked hit was drawn there, while the ticks showed them all.
- 2026-11-13: Phase 7.1 build choices, in `ai-docs/phases/phase-07.1/decisions.md`: the plan said Windows reports dropped watcher events, but the `notify` crate (8.2.1) cannot say so on Windows, so a burst of 64 or more events and every start-up reconcile stand in for it; own-operation updates sit in the Rust operations and not in `ops.ts`; the cache is written 500 ms after the last change; Quick Open follows the index while it is first built; the link scans and folder search list files from a root the index holds whole and walk any other. ISC-121 and ISC-253 now say the built-in excluded folders are not entered.
- 2026-11-13: refined: added ISC-313 and ISC-314 after the owner reviewed Ctrl+P: a search for "agents.md" listed `AgentSystem.md`, `multi-agent-support-plan.md`, `LeveragePoints.md`, `Max.md` (through a folder named agents) and others whose letters only sit in that order somewhere in the path. Settled with the owner: names only unless the query has a slash (as in Obsidian and VS Code), and the typed text must appear in the name as written; the old letters-in-order matching stays only as a fallback when no name has the text, so initials like `pln` still work (ISC-130). Phase 7.1, task P7.1-T4; see `ai-docs/phases/phase-07.1/decisions.md`.

The exclusions needed no change: ISC-282, ISC-294 and ISC-253 already keep the built-in folders and non-Markdown files out of every scan. - ISC-72: `pnpm perf scroll-stability` on the release build, six runs in a row on one build: the top line moved 0.47 px going up and 0.25 px going down every time (limit 2 px), while the control, an image widget with no reserved space, moved it 288 to 910 px. The sizes go in during the view's measure cycle and, when CodeMirror would not make up for the change, the first visible line is put back (`tests/editor/images.test.ts`, 10 deliberate breaks all caught, including the two-images-in-one-frame case that failed 6 of 6 runs before). Four earlier versions missed the limit; see ai-docs/phases/phase-04/decisions.md (P4-T2). Since 2026-11-11 the control is an injected 40 px jump (read back as 40 px over 20 steps); the real widget still moves 0.47 px up and 0.25 px down.
- ISC-72 (coverage note 2026-11-08, by me): the `scroll-stability` scenario walks its own image document, which has no table over 50 rows, so its pass does not cover the two-step table. That case was measured separately in the debug app and is in decisions.md, "ISC-6 fixed": with an idle-time swap the line under a big table moved up to 107 px; with the swap in the write phase it did not in 14 runs. This is debug-app evidence, not the release scenario.
- ISC-182: `tests/editor/images.test.ts` loads, fails and refuses an image and reads the same fixed height (160 px block, 20 px inline) before and after, and a remembered size reserves the real aspect ratio from the first frame; the real window showed the 160 px dashed placeholder with its alt text for a missing file (P4-T2).
- ISC-68: `pnpm test:web tests/editor/frontmatter.test.ts` (34 tests): the block at the very start of the text, `---` to `---` or `...` within 200 lines, is one pill labelled "Frontmatter" (it listed its top-level keys until ISC-238); a caret touching it, edges included, brings the YAML back as plain muted text and the pill returns when the caret leaves; typing the closing line far below turns it on; the block layer and the inline layer draw and style nothing inside it. The real window (debug app, trusted mouse) showed the pill `Frontmatter title, tags, draft` with the caret away and the plain YAML with the caret at the top; the first look showed the YAML styled as a heading by the highlighter, which is why open blocks now get a plain line class (P4-T3).
- ISC-69: `pnpm test:web tests/editor/checkbox.test.ts` presses a box and compares the file a save would write: one byte differs, the space or the `x`, for LF, CRLF, CR and a file mixing all three, and undo restores the file byte for byte, two quick presses on one box undoing one at a time. The real window (trusted click, Ctrl+S, bytes read from disk): one byte for an LF file (checked to open), a CRLF file and a file with 9 CRLF, 7 LF and 1 CR; Ctrl+Z then Ctrl+S gave the original bytes every time (P4-T3).
- ISC-184: the same file, a document made read-only through `Document`: two presses change no text, leave nothing to undo and the document unedited; the real window opened a file with invalid UTF-8 as read-only (`aria-readonly`), two trusted clicks on its boxes changed nothing and the file on disk was identical after Ctrl+S. 26 deliberate breaks across the T3 code were all caught; the first 21 left 6 alive, which led to a stricter scan edge, two redundant pieces removed and four tests tightened or added (P4-T3).
- ISC-70: `pnpm test:web tests/editor/codeHighlight.test.ts` (20 tests) opens fenced blocks in js, ts, python, py, json, css, sh, go and rust and reads the class each token gets from the style: keywords, strings, numbers and comments get four different classes, the block is plain until its parser has loaded, a fence word written in capitals works, the text of the block is the text of the file, and typing inside a block is colored again. The colors are `var(--syntax-*)` names only, and each exists in both theme files. Real window (debug app on the dev server, 2026-11-08): ts, python, rust, css, json and sh blocks colored by token, an unknown word and a fence with no word left as plain text, document length 702 equal to the file size; typing `7` after `42` in a block gave `427` still colored as a number and Ctrl+Z restored 702; raw mode (Ctrl+E) shows the same colors. Interceptor on `pnpm dev` through the mock host: the screenshot shows the same colors in the dark theme; in the light theme the computed colors are `oklch(0.48 0.15 310)` for keywords, `oklch(0.46 0.11 145)` for strings and `oklch(0.5 0.13 50)` for numbers, but the DOM-render screenshot washed them out so the light theme was checked by value, not by eye (P4-T4).
- ISC-71: `node scripts/check-arch.mjs languages-lazy --dist <build>` on a build made with `vite build --sourcemap`: the chunks the page loads first (the entry script, the modulepreload link and what they import statically, 2 files, 790 kB) hold `lang-css`, `lang-html`, `lang-javascript` and their grammars and no other language package, while the language chunks and the list of them load on demand. The claim as written, "no parser in the entry bundle", is not literally true and cannot be: `@codemirror/lang-markdown` imports `lang-html` (which brings `lang-css` and `lang-javascript`) for the HTML it finds in Markdown. The rule allows those six packages by name and nothing else; the allowance is a constant with a comment that says why. Source half: no static import of a parser package in `src/`. Runtime half: `tests/editor/codeLanguagesLazy.test.ts` counts imports of `@codemirror/language-data` and sees none for documents with no fence word, `~~~` with a blank word, or an unlabeled fence, and exactly one for two views holding three languages. In the real window the language list was not requested for a document with no fenced block and was requested after a document with one was opened. 9 deliberate breaks of the rule (ignore modulepreload, skip static imports, allow the list, drop `lang-css` from the allowance, widen the allowance, Windows paths, pnpm store paths, `explicit` flag, skip scanning) were all caught by `tests/scripts/checkArch.test.ts` (92 tests); 4 breaks of the editor code (no extension lookup, no `codeLanguages`, an unknown token name in the style, a static import of the list) were caught by the two test files (P4-T4).
- ISC-58 (closed again 2026-11-08, P4-T4 perf re-measure, owner approved the scenario change): on the T4 build the unmodified scenario stopped with "the line below the span is not in the view" because it read the view right after `forceParsing`, while the block widgets the parse draws had moved the line 199,000 px away until CodeMirror re-anchored. With `settleOnLine` (commit e564f1c) `pnpm perf reveal` on the release app: work per leave p95 4.10, 4.00 and 4.00 ms against 16 ms, five runs each, repeatable within 5 to 8 percent, and 4.00 ms (3.9 to 4.3) in the full named set on e564f1c. Phase 3 measured 3.7 to 4.0 ms. See decisions.md, "Perf diagnosis after your answers".
- ISC-63 (closed again 2026-11-08, P4-T4 perf re-measure, owner approved the scenario change): since T1 the same-work check had failed in every run, because the scenario read the view right after `forceParsing` and the two views showed different text (visible characters 4541 against 6605 at the late spot at T4). With `settleOnLine` and one fixed 5000-character scan range in both documents (commit e564f1c), `pnpm perf decorations-scale` on the release app: worst deviation 0.198, 0.192, 0.212 and 0.2155 against the 0.35 limit (an earlier settle variant 0.182, 0.210, 0.207), same work in both places every time, control 8 to 9 times. Phase 3 had 0.15 to 0.28. The limit was not changed.
- ISC-6 (reopened 2026-11-08, by me): closed earlier at 11.2 ms (see the entry above), and not true at HEAD. `pnpm perf tab-switch` on the release app, five runs each: switch p95 52.7 ms (51.6 to 53.3) on e564f1c, 49.6 ms (48.4 to 51.0) on d5166ab (T4), 52.4 ms (52.1 to 55.1) on 708a90c (T1), 16.1 ms on c98e4d0 (end of Phase 3); against 50 ms. The first build over it is T1, the block layer (T2 and T3 not measured: the bisect was stopped by low memory). The limit was not changed. See decisions.md, "Final perf set on e564f1c". Cause measured 2026-11-08: switching to the tables-heavy fixture draws a 2,907-cell table whole (51 to 56 ms median against 15 ms with the layer off); drawing only its first 40 rows brings it to 20 ms. Fix proposed in decisions.md, "ISC-6 tab-switch: profile in the real window", awaiting the owner.
- ISC-6 (closed again 2026-11-08, owner approved the fix, commits 4a1383d, 2fe9bdf and 9d6f8e9): a table with more than 50 body rows is built as its header and first 50 rows plus one spacer row at the height of the rest; after idle time the real rows are swapped in during CodeMirror's write phase, with a measure request keyed by the table's own wrapper (`src/editor/livePreview/blocks/widgets.ts`). `pnpm perf tab-switch` on a fresh release build of 9d6f8e9, five runs: switch p95 18.7 ms (runs 20.3, 18.7, 18.7, 18.2, 20.2; repeatable) against 50 ms; median 15.6 ms, plain 16.3, with a change made elsewhere 14.5, synchronous part 3.6 ms. Before the fix: 52.7 ms. An earlier run of the same code before the per-table key (2fe9bdf) read 19.7 ms and the harness called it not repeatable (spread 11%); it is superseded. The cause was measured in the real window: the first screen of `tables-heavy-1mb.md` is one table of 2,907 cells that the view laid out whole (51 to 56 ms median for that switch against 15 ms with the block layer off; 17 to 18.5 ms after the change). Tests: `tests/editor/tableProgressive.test.ts` (10 tests); I broke the widget ten ways on purpose (every row at once, spacer never replaced, no re-measure, no cancel on destroy, click ignoring the row, one row short, spacer too short, rest in reverse order, swap outside the write step, one measure key shared by all tables) and each failed a test. A real mouse click on a row not yet drawn landed on the right source line. Limit unchanged. See decisions.md, "ISC-6 fixed".
- ISC-71 (reworded 2026-11-08 by the owner's decision, closed again): the claim was "Language parsers stay out of the entry bundle", which is not literally true because `@codemirror/lang-markdown` imports `lang-html`, `lang-css` and `lang-javascript` (and their Lezer parsers) itself. The owner chose to reword it to what the check enforces: "The entry bundle holds no language parser beyond the ones Markdown itself imports (HTML, CSS and JavaScript); every other language loads when a fence names it." Evidence is the entry above: the check reads the source map of every chunk the page loads first and fails on any other language package; its tests plant a parser in the entry and watch it fail.
- ISC-185 (closed 2026-11-08, owner request with two Obsidian screenshots, top-level fences only by the owner's answer; commit c85e3c7; the owner then used it in the app and said it works fine): `src/editor/livePreview/box.ts` and a `FencedCode` branch in `scan.ts`. Each line of the block gets container classes and draws its slice of the border and background with `::before`. Seen in the debug app (an editor mounted over the app page, so the app's own CSS and theme tokens apply), screenshots in both themes: bordered, rounded, subtle border, gap above and below. Not seen through File > Open in a release build. Tests: `tests/editor/codeBlock.test.ts` (21 tests). See decisions.md, "Code blocks drawn as a container".
- ISC-186 (closed 2026-11-08): with the caret outside, the opening fence line and the closing fence are hidden and the first line carries `data-box-label` (the list's name, `TypeScript` for `ts`; the word as typed when the list does not know it; none without a word). Unit tests on the scan and on a real view's DOM, and the same in the real window.
- ISC-187 (closed 2026-11-08): a caret at the first character, inside, or at the end of the block shows both fences and drops the label; a selection that only touches the block does the same. Unit tests, and the real window (fences muted, no label, same box).
- ISC-188 (closed 2026-11-08): in the debug app the height of every line of the fixture, and the whole content height (771.9 px), were identical with the caret outside and inside the first block; no difference on any line. The fence lines are lines in both states.
- ISC-189 (closed 2026-11-08): `boxLines` takes a `kind`; tests call it with `mermaid` and get the same container classes, the label on the first line only, and lines for the visible range only (a 5,000-line block gives 51 lines for a 51-line view). A whole-element variant (`cm-md-box-whole`) is written in CSS but has never been drawn by a widget. I broke the code seven ways on purpose (last line as mid, fences hidden while shown, no visible-range limit, closing fence taken as the last mark, nested fences allowed, label on every line, one shared line decoration for different labels) and each failed a test.

Added after a check of what Phase 6 changed (T8 rewrote the session and hot-exit format, so ISC-47 and ISC-48 were run again, and the trusted-key typing scenario behind ISC-7), same release build, 5 runs: `pnpm perf crash-restore` restored 5 tabs and 3 unsaved buffers in 153.8 ms and "everything came back exactly as it was" (journal wrote 829 ms after the last edit against a 1000 ms interval); `pnpm perf typing-1mb-trusted` work per key p95 8.4 ms against 16 (repeatable, the worst key of a run was 14.8 ms at the median and 23.3 ms in the worst run). Not rerun: `journal-cost`, `asset-scope` and the 10 MB scenarios belong to open fog items, not closed claims; ISC-138 (`typing-during-search`) has no scenario yet and its claim is still open.

- ISC-97: `pnpm test:rust multiple_roots` opens a file in each of two roots and none outside them (src-tauri/src/workspace/roots.rs); `pnpm test:web tests/features/tree.test.ts` lists two roots with their own rows through the mock host, which keeps Rust's rule that only a folder inside a root can be listed. Broken on purpose (a removed root keeping its rows) and caught (P6-T1).

- ISC-194: `pnpm test:web tests/features/workspaceSession.test.ts`: two roots, chosen one after the other, and the open folders (three deep) come back through a restart, also when the session file is missing, damaged or from before the workspace; a remembered folder outside every root is ignored, and a snapshot that names a root adds none. - ISC-129: `pnpm perf typing-during-scan` (release build with the perf driver, Windows 11, AMD Ryzen 7 8840HS, WebView2 157.0.4341.42; the Windows display setting is 125% (AppliedDPI 120, read from the registry after the runs), but the report does not record the app window's own devicePixelRatio, so the scale the window ran at is not confirmed, commit a87d252 plus the T6 changes): work per key p95 8.90 ms across 5 runs (8.50 to 9.10, spread 4%, repeatable), against 16 ms; median 5.80, p99 10.0, worst key 24.5 (13.6 to 28.0). Every run planned a rewrite over 8,015 files (825 links) back to back, 25 scans per run at a median of 929 ms, with a scan running for 99.7% of the typing window. An earlier batch of the same scenario had p95 8.90 (8.40 to 10.0) but its first run finished no scan in the window and one run was 1.1 ms from the median, so it did not meet the repeatability rule; the overlap measure was added and the scenario run again, and nothing else about it changed.
- ISC-130: Ctrl+P in the real app (debug build, Windows 11, `real5`, 20 checks): opened from a CDP key and from a real OS Ctrl+P sent to the app window (the app was in the foreground, and WebView2 did not open Print), listed the four files of the workspace from the Rust index, put `plan.md` first for "pln", found `roadmap.md` for "planroad" (a pattern that reaches into the folder), showed "No file matches" for a non-match, never listed a file under `.git`, opened `plan.md` in a tab with Enter, showed a file made by another program once the picker was opened again, and closed on Escape and on a second real Ctrl+P. Screenshots: `pnpm dev` (browser mock host, tab driven in Chrome; not the Interceptor skill named in the claim): Ctrl+P listed the demo files, "rdm" showed `roadmap.md` and `README.md` with the matched letters marked, Enter opened `roadmap.md`; the real window screenshot shows the picker over an open document. Rust: 25 tests in `workspace::quick` (ranking, UTF-16 positions, limit and total, `.git`, refresh, lazy build, multi-root, cap). Page: 31 picker tests and 15 `fuzzy` tests.
- ISC-131: Ctrl+Shift+P in the real app (same run): opened from a CDP key and from a real OS Ctrl+Shift+P, listed the commands with their shortcuts (Toggle Sidebar Ctrl+Shift+B) without the two palette commands, greyed the ones that cannot run (Go Back, Go Forward, Save), "tsb" put Toggle Sidebar first, Enter ran it (the sidebar closed and the palette closed first), and Ctrl+Shift+B brought it back. Screenshot on `pnpm dev`: "tsb" showed Toggle Sidebar with its shortcut and the matched letters marked. Page tests cover a command that throws, one that cannot run, one that takes focus, closing before the command runs, and the shielded shortcuts.
- Breaks on purpose in the T7 code: 24 in the Rust index (each point of the score, the letter-order prefilter, case, spaces, the query cap, root names, the cap and its notice, rebuild on every query, `.git`, order for an empty query, total, limit, UTF-16 offsets, the name start, the path back through the table, refresh): 20 failed a test at once, 1 did not apply until its text was fixed and then failed a test, and 3 survived: the word-start bonus after a slash, the name start, and a rebuild on every query. The first two were fixed with new tests and are caught on a second run; the third is equivalent (a second look at the same cache returns the same index), so it stays. 39 in the page (scorer points, the query cap, shield, greyed command, stale answer, wrapping, Tab, Escape, Enter, backdrop, row reset on a new search, footer, refresh, failure, page keys, toggle, focus, rows, shortcut mapping, the mock): 31 failed a test at once and 8 survived (leading gap, word-start bonus, exact-case bonus, the query cap, a stale answer replacing a newer one, the row reset on a new search, PageUp and PageDown, focus taken back after a file opened) and were fixed with new tests; all 8 are caught on a second run. The first stale-answer test depended on timers and passed with the guard removed on one run; it now holds the slow answer with a promise it releases itself. The `palette-lazy` arch rule fails on a planted static import and on three planted tree variants (P6-T7).

- ISC-209: `pnpm test:rust save_as_read_only` and `tests/documents/saveAs.test.ts`: the copy is written, the notice and read-only state are gone, typing and plain save work, and the original is never the target. Real window: `latin1.md` opened read-only, Save As wrote `utf8.md` (UTF-8, no BOM, the accented letters kept), the Latin-1 original's sha256 did not change, the notice went away, typing marked the tab unsaved and a plain Ctrl+S saved with no dialog. Finding: only true Latin-1 comes out lossless; a file in another encoding mdmate cannot read (such as UTF-16 with no BOM) is shown through the same fallback, so its copy holds what was shown, not the original characters. Listed for the owner in decisions.md and untested.md.

The count runs once per change of query or selection and after a 250 ms pause in typing, so on a file this large a rare word costs about 25 ms of main thread per Enter in the box; typing during an open bar is measured by `typing-find-open` at the end of Phase 7.
